# openfilm

## 0.4.0

### Minor Changes

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Clips move. A clip's style may carry a CSS animation, its `@keyframes` in film.html's `<style>`: it runs on the clip's own time, moves the picture with `translate`, `scale` and `rotate` on top of its box (about its middle), and `--clip-length` times a way out from the clip's end. Agents write motion the way they write any CSS animation.

  In Studio, a clip's Transform (position, scale, rotation, opacity) works as in an animation editor: a stopwatch on a property sets its first keyframe at the playhead, and from then on a value changed (typed, or the picture dragged, turned or sized on the stage) sets a keyframe there; the stopwatch off removes its keyframes and keeps the value. A picked clip that moves opens its position, scale, rotation and opacity as rows under its track (a row's ◆ starts that one moving too), with its keyframes drawn by how they go in and out (linear, eased, held) and a navigator to step between them. Keyframes are picked by a click (⇧ adds), a row's name picks them all, a drag moves them by whole frames; F9 eases them (⇧F9 in, ⌘⇧F9 out), ⌥←/→ nudges a frame, Delete removes, and a right click offers the same. On the picture, the picked clip's frame and handles follow it where its keyframes have it at the playhead (dragging them keys there; Position & size stays its own box), and its position keyframes are drawn as a path: a line through every frame, a square at each keyframe, picked and dragged in place. ⌘C copies the picked keyframes and ⌘V pastes them on the picked clip at the playhead. The graph (⇧F3, or its button by the property names) draws each moving property's value over time: keyframes dragged there move in time and value, and a picked one's handles shape the curve into and out of it, written as the segment's own `cubic-bezier`. Cutting a clip keeps its motion going in the second half, a copy gets keyframes of its own, and every change undoes.

  The library adds 27 clip animations (12 ways in, 8 ways out, 7 loops, such as pop, whip, spin, blur, shake, heartbeat and a slow push) on the picked clips, and 11 camera moves on a cut (zoom through, pull back, whips in four directions, spins, flash zoom, blur, shake), the clip before the cut going out as the one after it comes in.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - A clip's grade is now standard color files: `--of-lut` takes OpenColorIO `.ctf`, Academy/ASC `.clf` and `.cube` files in order, evaluated as OpenColorIO 2.6 evaluates them. OpenFilm brings input transforms for sixteen kinds of footage (ordinary video, HLG, PQ and thirteen cameras' log) into ACEScct and the ACES 2.0 output to the screen, so a grade is written in one working space whatever the camera. While a graded copy is made, the preview draws the grade live; a copy holds only the stretch of the file the clip plays, and a clip graded and otherwise plain is exported straight from it.

  A video clip's color in the inspector is these files too: temperature, tint, exposure, contrast, highlights, shadows and saturation are written into the clip's own layer, and a filter of the library is a file made for the screen at the end of its chain, with its strength. Dragging shows the grade on the picture as it goes; letting go writes it, one step of the undo with the film. An agent's change to a color file shows at once, without drawing the whole picture again. ⇧D shows every video as its file is, and again graded.

  `openfilm color` is five commands for what an agent cannot do by writing a file: `look` measures each clip as it plays and its jump from the clip before, `input` says and sets what footage is, `balance` makes a clip's greys grey at a usual exposure, `match` brings shots of one scene to another's color, and `filter` lists the library's filters and puts one on. `show`, `set`, `auto`, `copy` and `reset` are gone: grades are files an agent reads and writes.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - The color workbench (beta, off until it is turned on in Plugins) is laid out as a grading page: the edit's picture with scopes beside it (waveform, RGB parade or histogram, a vectorscope with its targets and skin-tone line, and the middle in IRE, the white in kelvin and what is clipped, measured on the footage at its own depth as it is exported), a strip of the film's shots as they play graded, and a band of tools under them. On the left, a shot's grade as it applies, top to bottom: the footage's input, the layers it shares with other shots, its own, a filter or LUT for the screen, the output; the one picked is what the tools change. In the middle, basic sliders, shadows, midtones, highlights and overall wheels (each with its red, green and blue, and a reset), RGB curves, hue curves, and the library's filters shown on the shot; a white picker makes a spot clicked on the picture grey through the grade, and each page can be reset on its own. On the right, automatic balance, matching to a shot picked in the strip, copying a grade to shots, reset and export as a .cube LUT. Under the picture, the grade, a split with the footage on the left of a line, or the footage alone (⇧D). ← and → go from shot to shot in the strip, ⌘C and ⌘V copy a grade. Every change is the project's color files, one step of the film's undo, and an agent's change to them shows in the workbench at once: the shots and layers it changed light up, and a list of changes says who changed what. With a chat, shots and their color files can be handed to the agent.
  With nothing to grade the workbench says why: a film with no video footage (its picture made of web pages) is told what it grades and how to put footage in, with a way back to the edit; with shots and none taken, the steps of a first grade and a click to start on the first shot. Each page of tools says what it is for when pointed at.
  Pages and stills are graded too, by the same files: a film made of code has its shots in the strip, scopes and pictures drawn from its pages as an export draws them, and its grade drawn by the browser as an SVG filter, the same in the preview and the export (its layers in ACEScct from the picture as shown, without an input or output; hue curves and 3D LUTs are left out on them). Automatic balance, matching, the white picker and `openfilm color look`, `balance`, `match` and `filter` work on them as on footage. A shot's picture in the strip is dragged across to move through it.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - What comes from the community is kept in bounds. A project's own skills reach no agent until you allow them in that project, after reading them whole (invisible characters and hidden comments are marked, scripts are listed), and a changed file asks again. A workbench's page reaches no network address but the hosts its `openfilm.json` declares under `permissions.network`, and its page says which. A skill made by anyone but OpenFilm that brings scripts asks before it is installed or tried, and an update that would take more (scripts, hosts, a page, a program) asks before it is taken. Anything OpenFilm withdraws stops on every computer the next time the community index is read, wherever a copy of it is, and says why. Community items can be reported from their page. `openfilm publish [folder]` checks a workbench or a skill as the community's review will, and `openfilm://community/<owner>/<name>` links open the app on that item.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Publishing to the Community takes three steps in the app, with no sign-in to GitHub inside OpenFilm: Publish checks your workbench or skill as the community's review will (what is missing, what the scan finds), then reads it back from your public GitHub repository and compares it file by file with yours, then opens its listing on GitHub filled in, to submit. Cards and pages say how far an item is vouched for: Official, Reviewed (listed by the OpenFilm Community, every version checked and reviewed), or Unreviewed with where it came from. A maker's name opens a page of everything they made, and cards say how many people use an item. Community sources, at the side's foot, lists the indexes Discover reads: the OpenFilm Community always, and others you add after a plain warning; what another index lists never takes the place of an OpenFilm Community item, cannot withdraw anything, and asks before it is installed. Installing an OpenFilm Community item counts one install of that item, anonymously: only its id is sent (`OPENFILM_NO_STATS=1` turns it off).

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - `openfilm edit add <file…>` puts files of the project in the film: each a clip at the end of the track its kind goes on (a video or a picture on the last picture track, a sound on the last sound track, a new track when there is none), one after the other, and prints each new clip as a link, `film.html#<id>`. It is how the edit takes what an agent or another plugin hands it: the sender names the files, and where they go is the edit's to say. OpenFilm's own plugins bring their commands the way anyone's do, from a `package.json` in their folder.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - An editor's help is a dialog in the middle of the window, from the "?" in the editor menu or the ? key anywhere (not while typing): what its plugin says of it, then what the editor shows itself (Screening: how it is used step by step and its keys), then the keys between editors. Plugins add theirs with `help.set(editor, draw)`. Screening no longer has a help button of its own nor the sheet shown while ⌘ is held, and its speed and full-screen buttons look like the edit's. The cover's tile beside the main track is one size always, as tall as the main track at its lowest, and the main track starts a little taller. A new project's conversation opens on the question and a line of what to do, above the message box, with cards for the library and the Community.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Effects that move: a clip's (or a page element's) `filter` can read the effects' custom properties (`--fx-blur`, `--fx-glow`, `--fx-brightness`, `--fx-contrast`, `--fx-saturate`, `--fx-grayscale`, `--fx-hue`), which the host registers, so each eases by keyframes of its own (SPEC §1).

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - An element of a page clip can have a mask too, as a clip can, drawn in its own size and keyed as a clip's (an override's style, its keyframes `<clip>--e<hash>--mask` among its animations).

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - An element of a page clip can move by keyframes: an override's `style.animation` plays the film's own `@keyframes`, which the host puts in the page, on the page's own time like the page's own animations. Copies of the clip get keyframes of their own.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Studio's video export can meet a delivery's bitrate or file size (#367). Beside Standard, High and Master, the quality has Bitrate (the picture's average in Mb/s, peaks up to a fifth over) and File size (in MB, the sound included: the file stays under it, its picture's peaks held to the rate the size leaves). The dialog shows the rate a size leaves and refuses a size too small for the film's length.

  For H.264, HEVC and VP9, encoded on the processor (x264, x265, libvpx), never the graphics chip, whose encoders may run far over a rate asked, and in parts of five seconds at least, long enough for the rate to settle. HEVC needs an ffmpeg with libx265. Without a target nothing changes. Exports asked through Studio's API take `bitrate` (and `maxrate`, its peaks) in place of `quality`.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Exports of footage several times faster. A film's frames were each drawn in a browser and taken as a picture, footage
  too. Now a stretch that is nothing but footage is read straight from its file (on the graphics chip where it can be)
  and given to the same encoder: a clip that fills the stage, or stands in it with bars, with no effect of its own, and
  any captions over it, each line drawn once and laid over. Titles, effects, pages and anything else are drawn as before,
  and the two join frame for frame. Measured on a two-minute cut of 1080p footage with captions: 25 seconds, where it
  took 77.

  The picture is better too: the footage is encoded once, from the file, without the copy and the JPEG that stood
  between it and the film. Such an export is as sharp as its footage, so at the same quality its file is larger than
  before; Standard now gives the picture High gave, in a smaller file.

  Before any footage is read this way, a frame of each file is drawn both ways and has to be the same picture, so what
  you export is still what the preview shows. Footage a browser shows in its own way (HDR, ten bits) is drawn as before.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - A video clip can be graded by `.cube` LUTs: `style="--of-lut: url(looks/a.cube) url(looks/b.cube)"` applies them in order. The footage is graded at its own bit depth, before any browser cuts it to 8 bits, into a copy kept in the project's cache, so a dark gradient keeps the steps it would have at 10 bits; Studio's preview, `openfilm render`, `look` and exports all play that same copy. While a new copy is made, the preview shows the clip as it is for a few seconds. A clip without `--of-lut` plays exactly as before.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Version history is the project's, not the Edit's: its button stays on the top bar with the Canvas shown, with only the Canvas on, and with every plugin off, and saves, restores, copies and deletes versions there as it does beside the Edit. A plugin's workbench takes part through `provide`: `settle()` as the history opens and before it saves or puts files back, `restored()` once a version's files were put back.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - An Invert effect (`--fx-invert`, a percentage, as `filter: invert(…)`): a picture flashed to its negative on the beat, keyed as the other effects are (SPEC §1).

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - The library and the Community work as one, with packs. A pack is things put together: a music pack of forty pieces, or a mix of music, transitions and text looks. OpenFilm's own groups are its packs, always in the library; the community's are added whole from their page, where each thing can be heard or seen, starred or used on its own, and the whole pack fetched for working offline. An added pack is a line of its own in each library tab it has things in, under Added packs; a thing of the community is in the library once it or its pack is added, and taken out again from Mine. Every library tab ends with Find more in Community, and a right click on a thing opens its page there (its preview, the pack it is in). Packs can be starred, kept with the library's stars. Community catalogs may list packs (`packs`), each one maker's things in their order; older catalogs are read as before.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Links name a thing of the project, and Studio opens one wherever it comes from: `film.html#<clip-id>` shows the edit with the clip selected and the playhead at its start, `film.html#t=12.5` a moment, `film.html#t=3,6` a stretch, `.film/canvas.json#<node-id>` the canvas on the node, and a file no workbench shows is pointed out in the library. A plugin sends the person to one with `open(link)` from `openfilm/studio`, and its workbench says which files it shows (`provide`'s `opens`, patterns of paths in the project) and shows what a link to one names (`show`). A clip, a moment, a stretch or a node the app's chat points at now shows the workbench it is in, so a clip referenced while the canvas is shown brings the edit back. In the desktop app, a link to the project in an agent's reply opens it in Studio instead of a browser tab.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Media made with the OpenFilm service no longer passes through it where it need not. The files a run names go straight to the service's storage, at an address given for each file alone, and a clip a model made comes straight from where its maker keeps it, instead of both travelling inside the run and its answer. A service that does not offer this yet gets the files inside the run as before. With the service, Seedance takes its reference pictures by address too, and fetches them itself.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Media that was moved or renamed is found again without asking. Studio records each media file of a project by a
  fingerprint of its bytes. A linked file or folder that was renamed, moved to a folder nearby, or is on a disk that
  came back under another name is connected again, and a file renamed inside the project's own folder has its clips
  pointed at it. Studio says how many it found. For what it cannot find on its own, the viewer and the media pane say
  what is missing and take a folder to look in: everything found there is connected at once. A clip whose link leads
  nowhere is now marked as missing on the timeline.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - The assets drawer's Media has two places: This project (the media of the film you are editing) and Mine (everything
  of yours beyond this film, for every film). Mine shows what you keep there and the folders you make in it, to organise
  as you like; adding a clip or a file to Mine from the timeline puts it at its top. Upload imports files, taking no
  extra room where the disk allows, or links files or a whole folder where they are kept (a NAS, another disk), copying
  nothing; a linked folder opens with its own folders as it is on the disk, and taking away the link leaves it as it is.
  Other projects' media is not mixed in anywhere; to use something again in the next film, keep it in Mine. The
  projects window is back to My projects and Examples.

  In the other categories, what you starred is under Mine too.

  Your agent knows your media: `openfilm get my-media` searches Mine, through the folders you linked, by name, folder
  and prompt, says which projects use each file, and `--take <id>` brings one into the film. It is told to look there
  first, before OpenFilm's library and before making anything new.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Media a service made for the project now says how it was made. Pick a generated picture, clip, sound, piece of music
  or voice-over in the media pane and the inspector shows the prompt (copied with a click), the service and model, the
  rest of what was asked, and the files it was made from; something found on the web says whose it is and links its
  page. "Make another" asks for the same thing once more, under a new name. It is kept by the file's content, so it
  stays when the file is renamed or moved.

  Files asked for the same way, and a picture redrawn from one of them, are versions of one thing: the media pane shows
  them as one tile with their count, the newest on top, and the inspector lists them all to go back to any.

  Searching the media pane now finds generated media by the words of its prompt, in this project and in Mine.

  This covers what is made from now on, by your agent's `openfilm get`, the canvas or the voices pane. What a canvas
  made before says its prompt, service and model where the canvas noted them; other media made before has no record.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Media brought in from this computer no longer takes room twice. On the same disk it comes in as a file of the
  project's own that takes no extra room (on a Mac's disk, which Studio's copy did not manage before; elsewhere, the
  same file under a second name, which stays when the original is deleted). From another disk, media of 100 MB or more
  stays where it is and is linked, and Studio says so; smaller files are copied as before. "Link files…" still links
  whatever the size.

  Settings → General → Storage shows what the projects take on disk and how much of it is cache, with each project's
  share, and clears the cache: what the preview and exports made is made again when it is needed. Versions,
  conversations and the work itself are never touched.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - One way to point at a thing of the project, both ways: a link to it, with only what the web already has after `#` — an element's id, and a time and a part of the picture as W3C Media Fragments write them (`film.html#t=12.5`, `film.html#t=12,16`, `film.html#t=12.5&xywh=640,200,320,180`), the same `#t=` the film's sources are cut with. What the person points at reaches the agent as numbered links to where it is; the agent points back with Markdown links to the project, which the chat draws as the same pills (lit in Studio under the pointer, shown there when pressed), in place of the bracketed tokens it was taught before (those in kept conversations are still drawn). A link to a part of the picture opens in the workbench shown, which lights it. Plugins read links with `links` from `openfilm/studio`, and the plugin guide says how a plugin names its things.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - The library's Skills and Plugins tabs are one tab, Plugins, where Skills was. It lists OpenFilm's own plugins, the skills installed, the plugins people made and each agent's own skills together, with Installed, Favorites and Discover down the side, and chips across the top to show only workbenches, skills or connections. Each line shows what it brings (a workbench, a skill, commands, a tab of the library) beside its switch. A plugin's page and a skill's page say how to use it, what it brings and what it may do on this computer. Turning on a plugin with a workbench says where it now is, with Open to show it. A library arranged in Settings › Advanced keeps its order, with Plugins where Skills was.

  A plugin can now be a tab of the library instead of a workbench: its `openfilm.json` says `"tab": { "icon": …, "title": {…} }`, and its element (or page) is shown in that tab, after the library's own, while the plugin is on. These tabs are arranged with the others in Settings › Advanced.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - A skill or a plugin can bring commands. The `bin` of a `package.json` in its folder (npm's own field) names them, and `openfilm <name> <command> …` runs one, for your agent, for you in a terminal, and for plugins alike: this is how one does something for others that changing a file cannot do. A `bin` that is a single file is one command, run as `openfilm <name> …`. The command is given the words after its name, and `OPENFILM_PROJECT` (the project around the folder it was run in), `OPENFILM_PLUGIN` (its own folder) and `OPENFILM_RUN` (how `openfilm` is typed there); what it prints and its exit status are its own. Its page in the library shows its commands; while it is turned off they do not run. Bringing commands does not make a skill a plugin: a skill shows only in the conversation, and a plugin is one that also shows outside it, with a workbench of its own.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - A plugin's workbench can now be part of the app itself. The `browser` of a `package.json` in its folder (npm's own field for what a browser loads) names a JavaScript module whose default export is a custom element (a class that extends `HTMLElement`); Studio loads the module into its own page and puts the element where the workbench is, with `project-id`, `project` (where the project's files are read and written) and `folder` as attributes. Such a workbench is not in a frame, so nothing is out of its reach: its menus can open over the library, it reads what is dragged from the library, it inherits the app's colors as CSS variables, and it can call every request the editor itself makes. It runs with that much access, so only once you allow it on the plugin's page. A page (`index.html`) stays the way to make one that needs no such trust.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - A workbench plugin tells an agent the film as its own dimension has it with a `look` command: `openfilm <plugin> look [what] [t | a-b]` prints plain text an agent reads (its numbers with their units, what is wrong, what is right) and the path of each picture it saved under the project's `.film/look/<plugin>/`, or one JSON object with `--json`, and exits as `openfilm look` does (0 done, 1 failed, 2 not a project or a wrong command). `openfilm edit look` is the first: it is `openfilm look` as it is. The convention is in `plugins/README.md`; OpenFilm's own plugins follow it with `src/plugin-look.mjs`.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - A plugin can bring a program. The `scripts.start` of a `package.json` in its folder (npm's own field) is a program that stays running while the plugin's workbench is used, and the workbench shows what that program serves: the page and its program talk however they like, so such a plugin can do anything a program on your computer can (read and write the project, run `openfilm` and other plugins' commands, call a model). Studio starts it in the plugin's folder when its workbench is first shown for a project, with `PORT` to serve on, `OPENFILM_PROJECT`, `OPENFILM_PLUGIN` and `OPENFILM_RUN`, and with `node` and `openfilm` on its PATH, so nothing has to be installed. It runs only once you allow it on the plugin's page (off until you do), and is stopped, with all it started, when you turn the plugin off or withdraw that, and whenever Studio closes.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Anyone can make a plugin: a skill with a web page at its folder's root (`index.html`) is one. It is added as a skill is (a folder, a link, a project's own `skills/`), listed in the library's Plugins tab instead of among your Skills, and turned by one switch: on, your agent is given the skill and its page is a workbench you can switch to from the top bar, shown beside the library. The page runs on an origin of the plugins' own: it is told where the project's files are (`?project=`), reads them with GET, and writes them with PUT once you allow it on the plugin's page (off until you do). It cannot reach the editor, and a film's own pages still cannot write anything. Studio also remembers which plugin's workbench each project was left on.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - OpenFilm has a Community, in early testing: one window for everything people make and share, opened from the button beside the chat's "+" (Studio in a browser: Community in the OpenFilm menu, which keeps OpenFilm's groups and accounts in its own flyout, Join us). Its side starts with what is yours: Mine, what you have, by kind, with its switches (OpenFilm's workbenches, your skills, and the skills and connectors each agent brings, each let in or not), and Favorites, what you starred, kept with the library's stars. Then Discover's kinds, under its name; OpenFilm's own templates, text, stickers, effects, transitions, filters, sounds and music are there, marked official, a kind's cards a page at a time; and Publish at the foot. Every card opens a page showing it as it is used: a transition or an effect playing, a filter beside the picture as it was, a text style in words, a sound or a piece of music to listen to; Use in the library opens the library on its tab. Kinds are listed once and the list is open: workbenches, skills, connectors (the MCP servers your agents bring), and the library's templates, text, stickers, effects, transitions, filters, sounds and music, with the library's own marks. A thing added goes where it is used: the top right, the conversation, your agent, the library's tabs. Cards show a picture, what it is for, its kind and its button (Install, Turn on, or said Installed, On or In the library). Import, at the side's foot, takes a folder or a link and works out its kind. An item's page sums it up at the top (how hard it is to start with, how little work a result takes, what it costs, where you use it, who it is for), says what it is for and where to open it, and folds what it is made of under For developers. A plugin or a skill says these in its openfilm.json (`category`, `difficulty`, `ease`, `audience`, `cost`, `details`; plugins/README.md). Screening has a mark of its own.

  The large windows (Settings, Community, projects, export, version history) share one size and one look: the side on the left in the panels' color, the page on the right a step lighter in the dark (a step darker side in the light), and Esc pressed in one is its own.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Plugins are now a tab of the library, beside Skills, instead of a page in Settings. A plugin is one thing OpenFilm can do, for your agent and for you alike, under one switch: Edit (the film, its picture, its timeline) and Canvas. On, its workbench is shown and your agent is given its skill; off, neither. Every plugin can be off at once: a project then shows only its library, and your agent is given none of their skills. Your media is not a plugin: it is always there. OpenFilm's own system skills are found on their plugin's page and no longer have a switch of their own or a place among your Skills. Each plugin is a folder with its own description, so one more is one more folder. A plugin's workbench is a whole surface of its own, shown in place of the edit and the canvas: with more than those two on, the button on the top bar opens a menu of them.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - A plugin's element now reaches Studio through one public module, `openfilm/studio`, named by the page's import map: the project shown as a value it is told about as it changes (its name, folder, files and how they were made), its `film.html` read and written by clip id as the edit writes it, the toast, Settings, the app's chat (`refer`, `select`, `hover`, in the desktop app), the app's commands, files put into another plugin's workbench (`addTo('edit', [path])`) and what its own takes (`provide`), buttons of its own on the top bar (`topBar.set`), and its plugin's own server routes and events (`pluginApi`). `openfilm/studio/react` is the same for React, and the import map also names `react`, `react-dom`, `react-dom/client` and `react/jsx-runtime`, Studio's own copy. The Canvas uses only this module, as anyone's plugin does. How to make a plugin, and the whole interface, is in `plugins/README.md`. A plugin's element is now also told its workbench's name, as `plugin`.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Settings has a Plugins page: OpenFilm's workbenches (Edit, Canvas) are plugins you turn on or off there, in
  place of "Ways of working" under Advanced. A plugin's workbench shows, and your agent is given the skills that come
  with it, only while it is on: with Canvas off, agents are not offered the canvas skill and the manual no longer names
  it. One workbench always stays on, and a film made with a plugin plays and exports the same with it off. The choice
  is now kept for the desktop app, Studio in a browser and the CLI together; what you had chosen before is carried over.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Add My voices to the resource library: make a voice from a recording with OpenFilm's service, with Inworld (recommended) or ElevenLabs as its engine. The voice is its recording, kept on your computer and sent with each line spoken in it, so it keeps working when you sign in to OpenFilm again; removing it also deletes its clone at the engine. Simplify the voice navigation to Favorites, My voices and Providers.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Preserve subtitle positions, project fonts, backgrounds and effects in editing drafts. Styles that need browser rendering use a separate transparent layer, with an editable text backup kept hidden to prevent duplicate subtitles.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - A new workbench, Screening, for people who have never edited: the film plays large, as it will be exported, and is watched as on any video player: under it the film as a strip of its frames (a click goes there, a drag runs the picture along) and the player's row of controls — play, volume, the time; the speed, how Screening is used, full screen. Pointing at what to change is as plain: with the pointer on the strip, a row shows above it, where a click points at that moment and a drag at a stretch, the picture following; a drag on the picture circles a part of it. Each goes into the chat, numbered there and on the bar: a number goes back to it, its × takes it out. A mark and its pill in the chat point at each other: either under the pointer lights the other (a box's pill shows its box on the picture, whatever the moment), and either pressed goes to the place and rings the mark and its pill once. Scrolling or pinching over the strip brings the film closer, down to a second across it, for exact moments and stretches; sideways moves along it, and 0 shows the whole film again. The help button (or ⌘ held, Ctrl on Windows) shows how Screening is used, step by step, and its keys. It brings no skill of its own: it builds on the film skill, as the edit does.

  The top bar's workbench menu lists each workbench by name with its key: ⌥1, ⌥2… (Alt on Windows) show them in order, and ⌘E (Ctrl+E) goes back to the one shown before.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Skills are resolved as dependencies. A plugin, and a skill, name the skills they build on in their openfilm.json (`"requires": ["film"]`), and an agent is given the skills of the plugins on and what these require, each once, a skill after those it builds on; the system prompt's list and `openfilm skill` say what each builds on. How a film is written is its own skill, `film`, no longer the edit's: given while a plugin that changes the film is on (the edit, Screening), and not with only the canvas. No skill is given to every agent; the system prompt says the rest once, among it that what the person did not ask for or point at is theirs as it is. The edit and Screening bring no skill of their own, and `openfilm skill edit` still finds the film's.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - A film's sound is right by default. Export brings it to -14 LUFS (online video) unless you pick another loudness, and
  a sound export of the mix can be brought to a loudness too. Every render and export catches the peaks of sounds that
  add up past full scale, so a film never clips; stems stay as they are. The preview plays the film at the loudness it
  is exported at, through the same kind of limiter, and the meters show what will be delivered.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - What `openfilm` tells an agent is now a system prompt of a few lines (`SYSTEM_PROMPT.md`, in place of `MANUAL.md`): where the agent is, how to be with you, and that skills say how each kind of work is done. It no longer says what a film is or names any command for making one: that is each skill's, read when a task needs it, so an agent starts with far less to read. OpenFilm's own skills are listed after it, each by what its own description says, and only those whose plugin is on. The desktop app's own agent and the other agents it starts are given the same text.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - An element's words can animate part by part: an override's `split` (`{ "by": "char" | "word" | "line", "animation": …, "stagger": …, "start": …, "order": … }`) has the host wrap each character, word or line and give it the animation, the film's own `@keyframes`, at its turn (SPEC).

  A shape's stroke can be drawn on: how much of it is drawn keyed from 0 to 100% (`--fx-trim`, which the host registers with the effects), its dash as long as its longest path. An element's keyed opacity or effect keeps no static value of its own, which would stand over its animation.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Pictures in 3D: a clip that sets or animates `--fx-z` (toward the viewer), `--fx-rx` or `--fx-ry` (its turn about its X and Y axes) is placed so about its center, under the film's camera (a perspective as a 50 mm lens on the stage), which the film has once any picture of it is in 3D; a flat film is placed as it always was (SPEC §1).

  A video export can blur what moves as a camera's shutter does (motion blur, 90° to 360°): each frame the average of eight moments.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Add optional mouse preview, off by default: move the mouse to preview timeline frames without changing the playhead, then press Space to play from the preview position. Toggle it with S or from the ruler or empty timeline space's context menu, which shows its current state and shortcut.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Zoom the timeline with + (or =) and - while working in the timeline, alongside the existing Cmd/Ctrl shortcuts.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Add local video background removal and face retouching shared by preview and export. Bundle portrait/face models;
  provide optional checksum-verified BiRefNet subject extraction, on-device ONNX GPU/CPU inference, chroma key,
  image-guided edge softening, contraction/expansion and edge color cleanup. Retouching adds 17 skin, feature,
  geometry and makeup controls, configurable pigment colors, natural/portrait/makeup presets, original comparison,
  reset and undo. Small models work offline; the optional model downloads once with progress and retry. Quality
  modes analyze the requested frame, including reverse seeks and parallel exports, with bounded source-time caching.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Voice-over in two voices: a pair speaks a dialogue, written a turn a line. The voice library's voice-over category has Pairs beside Female and Male, with Doubao's podcast pairs and a way to choose any two Doubao voices; `openfilm get tts` takes two voices (`--voice a+b`, or `--voice` twice) and makes one recording whose transcript has a cue for each turn.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - A workbench can be laid out among the page's panels as the Edit is: its plugin's `openfilm.json` declares its `blocks` (with the Edit's viewer and timeline among them if it wants), Studio arranges them with the chat and the library, and the element draws each into its place (`openfilm/studio` `blocks`, `useBlock`). Each workbench keeps an arrangement of its own, shown, hidden, sized and moved from the layout's menu; the Edit's viewer, layers, inspector and timeline and the Canvas's board are blocks too, and the arrangements and named layouts kept by earlier versions are read as they were.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Each workbench says how it is used: the workbench menu at the top right has its title and a "?", which shows a card beside the menu with when to use the workbench shown and how (its plugin's own words) and the keys that go from one workbench to another; the card also comes by itself the first time a workbench is shown, out of the way of the workbench under it. Screening picked while the canvas was shown no longer leaves the page black.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - The edit and the canvas are now plugins like anyone's: each comes as the element its plugin brings (`plugins/edit`, `plugins/canvas`), and Studio's page puts in only the workbenches of the plugins that are on. With the Edit plugin turned off from the start, its code is not loaded at all; one turned off while in use stays as it was left, and comes back with its selection, playhead and undo steps when turned on again. The project's page itself (the top bar, the library, the layout, opening and reading the project, the app's chat commands) no longer depends on any workbench, so a page with only the canvas on, or with every plugin off, is complete.

### Patch Changes

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - The OpenFilm agent's model menu lists the service's models in their order: Claude Fable 5.2, Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5 and Haiku 5.5, GPT-6.2 Astra, GPT-6.2 Sol, GPT-6.1 Terra and GPT-6 Luna, Gemini 4 Carbon, Hy4 Flash and DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, each with its maker's mark. Subtitles translated with your own OpenAI key can use GPT-6.2 Sol and GPT-6.2 Astra in place of GPT-6.1 Sol and GPT-6 Astra.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - An agent the app runs reaches Studio with a key of its own: it reads the person's services and makes media with them, but connecting or disconnecting a service, its keys, the choices in Settings, Studio's preferences, and which plugins and skills are on, installed or let write are refused to it (only the person changes them, in Studio). When `openfilm get` makes something with a service or model other than the person's choice in Settings, its receipt says so (`madeWith`), and the media skill asks the agent to use the person's choice unless they ask otherwise, and to say which model it used.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Agents you run in the desktop app (Claude Code, Codex and the others) now get the system prompt meant for the app, as the app's own agent does: Studio is beside them, with how to be with you. Before, they were told the terminal's, which sent them to open Studio themselves. Nothing they read in the app mentions `openfilm open` or `npx openfilm` any more: Claude Code and Codex run the app's `openfilm` by its name, the others by its path, and a skill written for a terminal reaches them with the app's `openfilm`. How to point at the film (`film.html#…`) is now in the film skill and how to point at the canvas in the canvas skill, with one line in the system prompt on how links work in the chat. Screening now brings the media skill as the edit does. The media skill now covers only the library's music, sound effects and stickers, beside what services make. With every plugin off, an agent still gets the film and media skills, so it can make a film from the conversation alone.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Show “Connected” for an agent using your own key. Turn notifications now start switched off; an explicitly saved notification choice is preserved.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - An agent that swaps a clip for another and then takes that one out has it out. Its write was as near to the film before the swap as to its own swap, and on such a tie Studio took the older as the agent's copy, so the clip looked added since and stayed, though no one had added it but the agent: it came back each time the agent took it out, and the agent was told nothing. Ties still go to the person where the versions as near differ in a clip the person touched (or the order or the stage they changed); where they differ only in what writes did, the newest is taken as the copy, as an agent reads before it writes.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Check installed agents for updates in the background and from Settings. Show versions and update status in each agent's help, use its original installer or owning app, wait for running turns, and refresh model catalogs after a verified version change.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Add a shared asset library to the resource center, with stars, collections, original-location folder connections, offline records, previews, and recorded project usage. Distinguish adding media to a project from inserting it into the timeline, reuse existing project additions, and keep recorded references connected through library moves and folder reconnections. Share assets with the editor's project media panel, and load large libraries progressively.

  Use a persistent Windows Explorer-style navigation pane with two sections: pinned shortcuts and This PC. Keep the personal library as a real folder with its original hierarchy, and put locations, collections, recent files and smart views in This PC. Support pinning, unpinning, reordering, and navigation history, and merge the address, search, and file commands into one responsive toolbar. Place Examples in the project header tabs, browse project folders in the content pane, and share persistent large, medium, small, and detail views across projects and assets. Match the library icon to the chat toolbar and keep the inspector collapsible.

  Keep Projects and Asset library as permanent default locations, restoring them from older preferences and protecting their root folders from deletion. Allow every pin to be reordered and open the first pin by default. Add file-manager copy/cut/paste, paste-link and destination-folder operations for the library, with independent copies from connected folders, explicit original-location status and shared reconnection menus. Support creating library folders and connecting locations from This PC.

  Read only the currently opened directory. Connecting a folder records its entry without opening it, recursively crawling its contents, or probing its media; This PC and metadata views use the visited index. Read destination folders one level at a time, limit media probes to the rendered batch, and cancel pending work when navigation changes.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Keep model lists and each model's effort and speed options updated in a shared background cache. Agent settings now let you reorder and hide models, and OpenFilm follows the cloud catalog as models are added or retired.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - `openfilm canvas add <file…>` puts files of the project on the canvas the person has open, in a row below what is there, as the pair of `openfilm edit add`. A link to a node on another of the project's canvases (`.film/canvases/<id>.json#<node>`) shows that canvas first, then the node. The canvas shows sooner the first time: an empty board's double-clicking cursor starts moving a moment after the board is shown.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - A canvas group is an object like a card: picked or held it is on top with everything in it, and let go it stays on top, its layer kept with the board. A new conversation's chips put the Community first and the library after it.

- [#482](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/482) [`91a43ac`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/91a43acfe268e8e06d99249f085f76f4bbcd3393) Thanks [@rick-mingyu-liu](https://github.com/rick-mingyu-liu)! - Canvas: what a board made is listed newest first even when two saves land in the same millisecond.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - A new picture node on the canvas is square (and made square until another shape is picked), named "Picture 1" and renamed with a double click on its name; empty, it shows only its mark on a soft light. Its composer is laid out like a text node's: what it is made from shows as rounded tiles left of a "+" (upload, from the canvas, from the Assets), each pressed to show it or find it on the board, with a × off its corner; a tools button in its corner opens a row of a character, a style and a preset (a clip: a character and an effect). The model button shows its service's mark, the models' list no longer shows how long a model took, and the shape, quality and count read as one quiet line. The bar over a picture or clip node matches a text node's, starts with "Ask in chat", and shows on an empty one too (with Upload).

  The presets are pictures now: a picture node's presets open as cards, each a picture of what it makes, a kind at a time (story, characters, places, products, design, retouch), with those that need a picture wired in marked. Fourteen are new, from what the picture models' guides show people make most: an outfit change, an isometric diorama, concept art, a white-background product shot, an ad shot, an exploded view, a movie poster, a sticker sheet, an infographic, a video thumbnail, an old photo restored, line art colored, a picture made real, and a new background.

  A picture node being made is veiled by the moving field of dots, with a pill in its top left corner: how far it is (measured against how long that model usually takes for you, a turning ring until that is known), the time so far, and a stop. A new picture, clip or sound node makes with the model last picked for its kind. The tools row over the composer slides open and shut. A picture's bar now holds: change part, presets (the same picture cards), multiple angles, light (nine lighting looks, each shown on the same portrait) and tools (remove background, split grid, expand, upscale), each made as a new node from the picture. Split grid finds the panels by their gutters (or the sharpest seam), trims borders, offers the grid found, 2×2 to 5×5 or any grid up to 8×8, and lays the panels out in a group as they sat.

  Subjects: the character library is now a library of subjects, each a character, an object or a place, kept with its pictures and named with @ in a picture or clip node's words (an @ button and an @ typed open the list); the subject's picture is wired in and its name read as that reference picture. A picture's bar now holds change part, expand, remove background, presets, multiple angles, light, marketing, tools and an AI assistant. Change part brushes what to redraw, brushes what to remove (eraser), or marks the change with a pen, arrows, words and numbered pins, with undo and redo. Expand places the picture in a larger frame of any shape (or ×1.5, ×2), moved and sized, and fills only the new area through a mask. Light opens a relight window: a light dragged round a sphere, its color, brightness, softness and a second fill or rim light, beside the nine lighting presets. Marketing makes other sizes for each platform at once, splits a picture into its subject and a clean background, edits its text (the agent reads the text into lines to change), swaps a product, makes a product set (main, detail, in use, ad, features), a moving poster as a clip, or a store main image. Tools hold the eraser, mark-up, crop and rotate (shapes, straighten, flips), color adjustment (looks drawn from the picture, light, color, split toning, effects, compare), split grid, blend pictures, skin texture, ten expressions, a 360° panorama and upscale. The AI assistant writes, beside the picture, the prompt that would make it again, four ideas, or a six-shot list.

  The picture's bar is reorganized: Ask in chat, then Smart edit (formerly Change part), Expand, Remove background and Light; then Create (the preset cards, with what the picture can become in each tab: expressions, other sizes, a product set, a product swap, blended pictures, upscaled), Edit (crop and rotate, color, split grid, layers, all on this computer), Make a clip (moving, from this frame, to a last frame, with a camera move) and the AI assistant. A tool shows only when a connected model does it (Smart edit's ways, Expand and Layers need one that redraws part of a picture; Make a clip a clip model; the assistant an agent). Menus open their choices in place, under the item, instead of in a second menu beside them; expressions, sizes and a product set are picked first and made with one button. Upscale makes at the model's largest size.

  A node is named with its number among nodes of its kind (the first text is "Text 1" beside "Picture 1"). A picture, clip or sound being made shows on a surface of its own, lifted off the board. A node with several versions says how many in its name row, top right, and opens them there; the card no longer fans copies behind it.

  A making node referred to in the chat tells the agent the service, model and options set on it, and the canvas skill asks the agent to make it again the same way unless the person names another model. What the canvas history notes for a version an agent made is the model that really made it. The pieces a node is made from no longer show numbers in its composer, and its versions sit at the far right of its name row, after its size.

  Smart edit is reworked: the picture zooms (pinch, ⌘ and the wheel) and moves (Space and a drag), the brush is a ring its own size, an area is marked with a brush, a lasso or a box and can be turned inside out, the marks show as a tinted wash with a lit edge that can be hidden for a look, the share marked is shown, the markup adds a box and five colors, ideas start the words, and the model that redraws is picked there (the picture's own first). Cut out makes a card beside the picture that shows it being made; when nothing here cuts out, one press downloads the free cut-out model that runs on this computer and cuts it out. Every card being made, a note being written or a file being brought in, sits on a surface of its own, off the board. A picture's or a clip's card is resized only in its own shape. The picture bar's menus open as tiles in their own color with what they share at the top, the grid cut shows its grids drawn, and the create cards and the tool windows' choices are finer.

  Smart edit is one canvas with its tools along the foot of the picture: select (brush, lasso, box, the subject found on this computer, eraser; inverted, grown or shrunk) and mark (pen, arrow, box, words, numbered pins, in a color); what is sent follows what the model takes, said under the words (the marks, the selection, or the words over the whole picture). Its words sit in a composer like a node's, with the picture, pictures to take from, the model, and a send button. Expand sets its canvas as a drawing program does: edges and corners dragged out, its size typed in pixels with the shape kept or free, snapping to a grid and to the middle; what the model makes at its nearest shape is cut to that canvas, at that size.

  Smart edit is simpler: the picture large, a few tools over it (brush, box, lasso, the subject, eraser, numbered points, the brush size, undo; inverting, growing and shrinking under More), and one input under it with pictures to take from, the model and send (Enter). Expand gives only the widened picture: one node beside the picture, made from the picture and the mask, cut to the canvas set; the in-between picture is no longer put on the board, and the picture now always reaches the model.

  Smart edit takes pictures to take from as a node's composer does (tiles, and a + to upload one or pick one of the project's), and its zoom sits in its tool bar. A canvas window darkens and blurs the board behind it, and the bars round the node give way while it is open. The library shows every file as itself: versions of one thing are no longer stacked into one tile.

  What a picture tool makes is named after where it came from ("Picture 1_Expand", "Picture 1_Edit", "Picture 1_Cutout"; a number after one taken). Stopping a picture, clip or sound being made stops waiting for it: what started is charged and cannot be called back, so it finishes into the project's files instead of being lost, and the node can be made again at once. Select subject works (what a service says while it works no longer breaks reading its answer), the brush in smart edit follows the pointer without lag, and the picture windows are larger and titled alike.

  What the canvas makes on the way to a picture (masks, marks, a picture framed to widen) is kept in assets/.canvas, which the library never lists, and what was only on the way (a subject cut out to select, a widened picture before it was cut to size, the framed picture) goes to the trash once used. Expand picks its model, and says when the one picked cannot widen a picture. The relight window is laid out anew: the picture in its sphere large, presets or the light set by hand beside it, and the person's own words with send below; a preset picked is made at once, as relit pictures, create cards from a picture, expressions and upscales now are.

  The style library shows each style as a picture of one same scene made in it, not a color and its words.

  A picture's and a clip's tools start with a camera: four wheels (the camera, the lens, the focal length, the aperture), each with its picture and the lens's look shown above, its words wired into the node; subjects are named with @. @ lists what is in the node already and brings a subject or a picture; what goes in is dragged into its order. The styles show each a scene of its own.

  The camera has more of each wheel (18 cameras, 14 lenses, 11 focal lengths, 10 apertures), each camera and lens drawn on a clear ground, and every one shown on one same street as it would make it, the wheel being turned leading what is shown. There are 46 styles in five kinds, art movements among them, each its own scene. The tools wear pictures of their own. The canvas's pictures are small WebP files.

  A style, a camera move and a camera picked for a picture or clip are kept on it, not put beside it as text: its tool shows what was picked, lit, with a picture of it when pointed at and a × to take it off, and their words go in when it is made (as a preset does). The composer keeps one color with its tools open or closed, its menus all open upward, and the style tool has a new picture.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - An official plugin is one package: the skills of Edit and Canvas now live in their plugin's folder
  (`plugins/<id>/skills/<name>/`), and `openfilm canvas` is the Canvas plugin's own command. It is typed and answers as
  before; with Canvas off, it says the plugin is off, as any plugin's command does.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - The canvas's toolbar adds a node with one click: text, a picture, a sound, a clip or a cut, each in view and clear of the others, beside the libraries and upload. A small bar at the bottom left, matching the one at the bottom right, holds the keys (their panel opens in the middle, over the toolbar) and one button, always lit, that switches between select and the hand; a switched-on button in either bar now has a clearly lighter ground. A double click on the board opens the add menu every time (the press that closes a menu is not half of one), and the canvas's menus (add, right-click, libraries, zoom, text style, alignment, colors) share one size and look. An empty canvas says "Double-click the canvas to add a node", the toolbar's buttons sink and spring back when pressed, a new node comes in with a small pop, and a double click on the board leaves a ring where it was. The Beta tag is gone.

  A text node is a document now: empty it says "Double-click to write"; written in, a bar over it sets a heading or body
  text, a bulleted or numbered list, a quote, the alignment, bold, struck, italic and underlined words, undo and redo,
  and opens the note large in a window of its own, with its name as a Markdown file to download. Selected, its bar sets
  its background color, opens it large or downloads it. Its words stay Markdown (underline and alignment as the HTML
  Markdown allows), and its color is kept in the canvas file. Its panel's "+" adds references to write from, and the
  four quick starts are gone. A text node is named "Text 1", not "Text node 1", and a double click on its name renames it, on the board or in its window, whose top is a title bar clear of the window's buttons that drags the window. Selected, its words scroll; they show no spelling marks. The bars over and under a node come in with a slight rise or drop, a node's name (and its rename field) is as wide as
  its words up to a limit, and a note the agent writes is veiled whole (what it said before out of sight) by a field of dots with a wave of light
  running through them until its first words come, which then stream in with a caret at their end; a "Writing" pill on the card says since when and cancels it.
  What it said before is kept: its versions are listed in the name row over the card, one picked takes the place of what it says now,
  and an earlier one can be deleted. What a note is written from shows in its panel as rounded tiles left of the "+",
  kept after sending: a note wired in as its kind's mark (pressed, the note is found on the board), a file added as
  itself (pressed, shown large when it can be, a Markdown or text file read as a note's words); a × off each tile's
  corner takes it out. With words already in it, the panel asks how to change them (they go with the ask, to rework).
  The bar over a selected note, picture or clip starts with an "Ask in chat" button: the node becomes a reference in the
  chat's message, named as the board names it (kept short), for the agent to change or make from.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - A project's page no longer starts downloading the canvas's code at its first paint, beside the edit's own: the canvas is
  warmed once the project has opened, as before. The Projects window, with no project open, still warms it for the
  examples' canvas previews.

- [#494](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/494) [`5899535`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/589953539de2c8c8c119e29f9c6033c1e90ed1fd) Thanks [@rick-mingyu-liu](https://github.com/rick-mingyu-liu)! - Correcting text within one caption word or styled span keeps its word timing and inline formatting.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Caption tracks no longer go missing when the agent writes film.html while you edit in Studio. Merging the agent's write with your edits used to drop every caption track (its `<track>`), whoever had added it; Studio then took the track as one you removed and never put it back. Each caption track is now merged by its file like a clip: one you added, hid or removed stays as you left it over an older copy, and one the agent adds, renames or removes from the latest copy is taken.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Smaller exports from the graphics chip, with a better picture where it is hard. H.264 and HEVC at Standard and High
  were given a fixed bitrate on the chip, so a film of slides, motion graphics or a screen recording came to two or three
  times the file it needed, and detailed footage was starved. The chip is now held to the quality instead, as the
  processor's encoders are: each frame gets what its picture needs. Measured on Apple silicon: the same picture in files
  50 to 65% smaller for simple pictures, and detailed footage at about the size it was, without its worst frames. A film
  busy in every frame (grain, static), which would come to twenty times its file, is held to once and a half of it.
  `openfilm render` does the same for its MP4. A chip that cannot be held to a quality keeps its bitrate.

  On Windows, a computer whose graphics driver has no Vulkan now draws through Direct3D on its graphics chip instead of
  on the processor. When the frames are still drawn on the processor, the export says so while it runs.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - The Community is laid out anew: plugins as rows with their icon, name, summary, publisher and action, in one or two columns as the window allows; skills as cover cards in a grid; the library's kinds on one sliding line; examples as cards without the coloured banner; quieter section heads with "See all ›", dashed empty states, and a bordered call to publish. An editor's page opens on a short recording of the real editor at work (`showcase.video`, served from the plugin's folder); a skill's page on its request being typed and carried out in the chat; a connector's page has no picture band.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - The Community is laid out as a store of extensions. Its side, under a plain Search: Mine, then the Community's Plugins, Skills, Library and Examples, and at its foot Publish and Add (import a folder or a link, or another catalog to read).

  Everything is got before it is yours, then managed. A card in the store says Get, or Installed, or Off. A page opens with its icon large, its name, who made it and what it is for, then what to do: Get; once got, Turn off or Turn on, and Uninstall (a skill also Try now and Update); its facts (kind, maker, users, version, scripts, source) at its side, then its preview, what it does and how to use it. OpenFilm's built-in plugins (Screening, Edit, Canvas) can be turned off, not uninstalled. Mine lists only what you got: your plugins in rows you drag (or move with Option and an arrow) into your own order, which the top right's switch and its shortcuts follow, your skills, and your packs, each managed from its gear (turn off, see details, uninstall); an agent's own skills and connections are folded in a line each.

  Plugins are of two kinds, editors (a way of working on the film, picked at the top right) and connectors (a service your agent reaches). Skills are shown as cards with their cover, playing their preview under the pointer, a grid for each kind of work and a chip to see one kind. The Library shows each kind's packs by their own pictures, a chip to see one kind with all its things; a thing opens large over the page, stepped through with the arrows, with its pack, maker and licence. Packs are got whole and said Added; a pack the Community has a newer version of says so, and Mine updates it; packs can be removed from the library, and OpenFilm's own removed ones are put back from Mine. Media packs (footage, pictures, recordings) are saved as a folder of yours under Mine, named after the pack. Examples, whole projects made with OpenFilm, are a kind of the Community like any other, kept in their publishers' repositories and read from the Community's index with its news; one opened is copied into a new project from the projects window. Every plugin and skill has an app icon (a square icon.svg, icon.png or icon.webp at the root of its folder); one without gets the default plugin or skill icon.

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Connectors: your agent can reach the services you work in. Six come with OpenFilm, in the Community's Plugins under Connectors: Dropbox (find and fetch your files), Frame.io (review comments, upload versions, fetch media), Notion (scripts and shot lists from your pages, and back), Adobe (Express, Firefly, Photoshop and Adobe Stock tools), Canva (find and make designs, such as covers) and Vimeo (upload films, edit their details, read their analytics; beta). Get and connect one and its service's sign-in page opens in your browser; sign in once and every agent in the app can use it, OpenFilm's own as well as Claude Code and Codex, with no more sign-ins. Its page says whether it is connected, offers things to say (a click puts one in the chat), how it works and what it can do, reading apart from changing; before any agent changes anything in a service, OpenFilm asks you (allow once, or while OpenFilm is open), whatever that agent's own permissions; a tool call shows in the chat by its service ("Notion · search"). What you signed in with is kept sealed in your system's keychain and refreshed by the app, never handed to an agent: each reaches the service through the app. Disconnect, turn off or uninstall from its page or from Mine. A plugin is a connector when its folder has a `.mcp.json` as Claude Code plugins have (`{ "mcpServers": { "<name>": { "type": "http", "url": "https://…" } } }`).

- [#496](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/496) [`bd1359c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd1359c40dc479f05e6867cb50ee434a9526802c) Thanks [@riverisbuilding](https://github.com/riverisbuilding)! - Studio: the main track is never lower than its cover needs; picking another moment for a cover keeps the film's page open a minute instead of opening it again each time; the Community's library says it is empty for now and fills up over 0.4.x; examples show who published them (OpenFilm with its tick); the projects window's version card is one button to what is new.
