- [#109](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/109) [`5f8b70f`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/5f8b70f8a453dc10fe3c86dd103c420473051fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - On macOS, an update that is ready now installs when you quit even if you open OpenFilm again right away. Opened while the update is still being installed, OpenFilm waits for the install to finish and then opens as the new version. If an install did not happen, the next launch installs it in a small window that says so, then opens as the new version. The update notice says what happens when you quit.

## 0.2.1

### Patch Changes

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - An agent's menu shows each choice once. GitHub Copilot lists its model "Auto" twice, and both showed, ticked.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - The chat takes any number of files in one message, and folders too. A file or folder dragged in from your disk stays where it is: the message gives the agent its path, and the agent copies into the project only what the film uses, so Studio's media no longer fills up with everything you dropped. A file pasted from the clipboard, which has no path of its own, is kept in the project's `.film/attachments/`. Up to 20 pictures go to the agent with a message, the pictures of what you pointed at in Studio first; the rest go by their path.

- [#75](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/75) [`6a82e79`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/6a82e7987cd7174739a2aba62513880573fe3216) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: files imported together into a folder that is not there yet all land. One of them could be refused as "not a path inside the project" when another import made the folder at the same moment.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: ⌥-drag copies, paste, ⌥←/→ and a start time typed in the inspector no longer cut clips. A copy or paste lands on a new track when its spot is taken; a nudge stops against the next clip. A new track opens above a picture track and below a sound track, so the track you pointed at keeps its number.

- [#68](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/68) [`c164fee`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/c164fee480a36fa5f70885d80df1a404ce3b5764) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a picture's `url()` left with an open quote in a page's changes no longer stalls the editor.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Footage is decoded on the graphics chip on every system: VideoToolbox on a Mac, Direct3D 11 (then NVIDIA's decoder) on Windows, and VA-API (then NVIDIA's) on Linux. This covers the fast-seeking copies, the preview's proxies and the files an editing project converts. Each decoder is tried once on a moment of H.264 and used only when its frames match the processor's pixel for pixel. If it fails partway, the work is done again without it.

  Footage the render's browser can't decode no longer stalls an export: ProRes, which no browser plays, and HEVC on a computer without a chip for it. Before rendering, such footage is converted to a copy the browser plays, keeping its transparency when it has one. A film with ProRes footage used to take about thirty seconds a frame and showed none of it.

  `openfilm doctor` says which encoders and decoders ffmpeg uses on the chip, and which video codecs the browser decodes there.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - A diagnostic report to paste into an issue. `openfilm doctor` checks what OpenFilm needs on this computer: Node, ffmpeg, the browser films are drawn in and whether it uses the graphics chip, Studio, the data and projects folders, and npm. It also shows which agent ran the command and the last errors in Studio's log. Settings → General → Copy report copies the same report, plus what the browser Studio is open in can do. In the desktop app, it adds each agent's program, version and state. Paths in your home folder show as ~; the report has no keys, accounts or projects.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Diagnostics move to Settings → Developer, which Studio now shows outside the desktop app too. Studio and the desktop app keep what went wrong (an agent that did not start or answer, or whose process ended by itself; an export that failed; an error Studio did not expect) in `logs/problems.jsonl` in Studio's home: the last two weeks, at most 500, with home paths, keys, tokens and email addresses taken out. The copied report (and `openfilm doctor`) ends with the last week's problems. Export writes a diagnostic file to the exports folder: the report, the problems, the end of each log, how the failed turns went (what was said only if you ask for it), and Codex's own warnings and errors around its problems. Settings → Developer lists the recent problems. In the desktop app, a message that failed has "Copy diagnostic report" beside its error, and so does an agent with a problem in Settings → Agents.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a file dragged from the media pane onto the timeline is put down where you let go, or on a new track when that spot is taken. It never cuts, pushes or covers a clip there; hold ⌥ to overwrite.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Exports leave the computer usable, and do less work twice:

  - **Fewer browsers.** A render opens one browser per performance core minus one, as many as the memory free right now can hold with 2 GB to spare, and at most four. Before, a Mac with 16 GB opened five and could run out of memory. A short render opens one.
  - **Lower priority.** Exports and `openfilm render` run below the apps you're using, along with every browser and encoder they start. This now holds in the desktop app too.
  - **Your film's frame rate.** A platform preset exports at the film's own rate, up to the most the platform shows. A 30 fps film is no longer drawn at 60 for 哔哩哔哩 or YouTube.
  - **One render for same-size presets.** Platforms that take the same picture, such as 抖音, 快手 and 视频号, are drawn once and their files cloned.
  - **Faster PNG frames.** Alpha videos and editing-project exports take their PNG frames with fast compression. The pixels are the same, and the frames come two to four times sooner.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Exports leave more of the work to the Mac's media engines, so the computer stays usable while they run. ProRes exports, and the pages an editing project carries, go through the ProRes engine, about three times as fast with a quarter of the processor. `openfilm render` writes H.264 on the graphics chip, as Studio's exports already do. The copies a render makes of slow-seeking footage, and the preview's proxies of heavy footage, are decoded and encoded on the chip too. H.264 and HEVC from the chip get a key frame every two seconds instead of every twelve frames, which looks better at the same size. Where the chip can't do something, the processor does it as before.

- [#90](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/90) [`91b4e90`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/91b4e90c13be6285b03534dfbafe1f028b29d5cc) Thanks [@taavie-z](https://github.com/taavie-z)! - Keep footage audio connected to its own picture in Final Cut Pro exports, preserving source trims, volume and disabled audio. Export separate sound as audio-only clips.

  Match exported audio roles to Studio’s displayed row numbers, including overlapping clips and empty rows, and assign dialogue, effects and music roles to mixed stems. Keep Voice and Footage in separate dialogue subroles so they can be soloed and exported independently.

  Write audio volume keyframes without unsupported interpolation attributes so Final Cut Pro imports faded audio correctly.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - A file whose name has `#`, `?` or `%` in it now plays in a film. Before, `#` made film.html fail to read, and `?` or `%` stopped the preview with an error, or left the file out. film.html writes those three characters escaped (`%23`, `%3F`, `%25`); every other character, Chinese punctuation and spaces included, is written as it is. A src written with escapes, like `my%20file.png`, now names `my file.png` in Studio too. A film's versions also keep such files, and files with quotes in their names.

- [#61](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/61) [`a5b0917`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a5b0917de6bb2fcb490bb65cb957d6c9eadf2c19) Thanks [@taavie-z](https://github.com/taavie-z)! - Export editing projects for Final Cut Pro as FCPXML with their media, the main picture track on the primary storyline, connected overlays and separate audio clips or mixed stems. Separate audio clips retain Studio's row grouping with an audio role per row. HTML pages render as ProRes clips with alpha; subtitles are included as editable title clips above the picture tracks with a separate SRT copy.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio's timeline: the magnet keeps the main track (the lowest picture track) closed up. A clip put on it goes in between, a deleted one closes the gap, and a trim or a new speed moves what follows. Linkage keeps the titles, overlays and sounds over a main-track clip on their moment of it, and deletes them with it; a clip that starts past the end of the main track rides on nothing. Dragging the cut between two clips trims the clip you grab. The new preview axis shows the frame under the pointer as it moves over the timeline; its switch sits in the toolbar beside the magnet, snapping and linkage. Keys: P (or ⇧N) magnet, ~ linkage, S preview axis.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - The main track's head no longer carries a magnet badge, which read as a switch: the main track is told by its band and its tooltip, and the magnet itself is the switch in the timeline's toolbar.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - A web page picked in the media pane now plays as smoothly as the film in the viewer. The page draws its own frames, one per frame of the project's frame rate, instead of waiting on a message from the editor for each one, so a busy editor no longer makes it stutter. Pausing stops on the frame on screen, and the timecode and the position bar match it.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a clip dragged onto another clip no longer cuts it. It lands where you let go, or on a new track right above when that spot is taken. Hold ⌥ while dragging to insert.

- [#83](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/83) [`5c2000b`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/5c2000b8cd049074f7944e31b8d991d8dfc69bea) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - `openfilm capture` is gone: an agent takes a picture of a live web page with its own tools, so OpenFilm's core leaves it out. The keynote and screen-demo skills no longer point to it.

- [#87](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/87) [`bd81148`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/bd811486aff50f0eeea33a8bc6e196a994bf8ded) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: Gemini CLI is no longer one of the chat's agents. Google stopped serving it to personal accounts on June 18, 2026; Gemini models are still there through Your own key and as a media service.

- [#88](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/88) [`893641d`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/893641de9e901f593b0dd4a75ac0dcb03cb8e3e8) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: each of your own models takes its context length in Settings, so the agent compacts before the model's own limit instead of at 128K.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio's preview draws web pages at the size the viewer shows them. A page that sizes its canvas or 3D by the device pixel ratio, as the protocol asks, now draws only the pixels you see instead of the film's full size scaled down, so heavy pages play more smoothly and look the same. Going fullscreen loads the film again behind the picture at the new size. Turn it off under Settings → Playback → Draw pages at the viewer's size. Exports always draw at full size.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio renders what plays late. While the film plays, Studio notes where the viewer can't keep up, such as a page heavy with 3D or many videos at once. Once you stop, it renders those stretches in the background at low priority, and from then on they play smoothly from the render. Paused, scrubbed or edited, the viewer still shows the live film, and any change to the film or its files renders them again. A line under the timeline's ruler marks each stretch: red plays late, yellow is rendering, green is rendered. Turn it off under Settings → Playback → Render what plays late. Exports always draw the film itself.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - A project can have a cover of its own in the projects list: pick a picture from a project's "…" menu, use the frame under the playhead from the editor's "…" menu, or set one of the project's pictures from the asset pane. "Use the automatic cover" goes back to the film's own picture.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Projects now shows every project in your projects folder, one folder each, including the ones an agent made there or you copied in; one you delete from the folder leaves the list. Projects you opened from elsewhere stay in the list too, and a project's … menu has Reveal in Finder (Explorer on Windows) to show where its folder is. Settings → General → Storage calls it the projects folder. When you change it, or set it back to the default, Studio asks whether your projects move with it: moved, each folder goes whole (across disks it is copied, then the old one goes to the Trash); left behind, they stay where they are and in the list.

- [#63](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/63) [`4802dfd`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/4802dfdaa27ed30ff466978fe9bc7ac833ee4644) Thanks [@zhoushoujianwork](https://github.com/zhoushoujianwork)! - Open Settings from the native menu with Command-comma on macOS or Control-comma on Windows and Linux, including when no project is open.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Any tab can rename its project, not just the one in front: right-click it and choose Rename, or double-click it. A tab's name being edited now shows its selected words plainly.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio's preview plays smoothly and in sync. The film now draws its own frames while it plays, once per frame of the film's frame rate and timed to when each frame reaches the screen, from a clock that follows the sound as you hear it, output latency included. It no longer waits on a message from the editor for every frame, so a busy editor or a slower computer no longer stutters the picture. Videos play by themselves and are eased back into step instead of being paused and sought again and again. Footage inside a web page that seeks its video frame by frame now plays at its full frame rate while the film plays, and still lands on the exact frame when you stop. The editor does far less work while playing: the playhead moves on its own, and the timecode and subtitles update only when they change. Changing speed, soloing a track or editing during playback no longer stalls the picture. Exports are unchanged.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Dragging the playhead along the timeline is smoother. The picture follows every frame instead of 25 times a second, and so does the timecode under it. The playhead line moves without making the editor lay its whole page out again, and the rest of the editor updates a few times a second until you let go. Zooming the timeline no longer redraws every waveform at each step: each one is redrawn once the zoom stops, a few per frame, so a film with hundreds of sounds doesn't freeze when you zoom.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Footage the browser can't decode now shows everywhere a film is drawn, not only in exports: `openfilm look`, posters, stills and slides, Studio's film cards, and page thumbnails. This covers ProRes, and HEVC on a computer without a chip for it. Each place draws it through the same copy an export makes. Studio's preview also plays that copy when no proxy is ready. A video exported with its subtitles burned in gets these copies too; before, its ProRes footage came out black.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Right-click a tab for its menu: rename it, show its project's folder, close it, close the other tabs or the tabs to its right. Renaming a tab now shows the selected words clearly. The chat's agent menu has a settings button at its top right that opens Settings → Agents.

- [#70](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/70) [`4934641`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/4934641f649d2f9dc992ecaceb62709485f8708a) Thanks [@GZUCMHjy](https://github.com/GZUCMHjy)! - Studio: a preview the player cancels closes its file, so a project can be renamed on Windows.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Zooming and scrolling the timeline of a big film stay smooth. Only the tracks near the view have their clips drawn, so a film with hundreds of sounds on dozens of tracks lays out a fraction of them. A pinch or Ctrl+wheel zoom scales what is drawn while you zoom and lays the timeline out once you stop, keeping the moment under the pointer where it is. Scrolling up and down moves the track heads with the tracks without redrawing the timeline.

- [#93](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/93) [`a7faee0`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/a7faee013da49aa8415bad2da9f58be26e093fab) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: scrolling a long timeline sideways is smoother. Keeping clip names in view no longer restyles every clip on each step.

- [#72](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/72) [`c97b30d`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/c97b30d553d2210cb233eaeff2aea00fef0d3e18) Thanks [@HEOJUNFO](https://github.com/HEOJUNFO)! - A subtitle line with `&`, `<` or `>` reads as written in an exported WebVTT file. A line like `Tom & <Jerry>` lost `<Jerry>`, which players read as a tag, and a speaker's `&` broke the voice tag; they are now written as WebVTT's `&amp;`, `&lt;` and `&gt;`, as the voice-over captions already were. `-->` in a line stays `-->` instead of turning into `→`.

- [#74](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/74) [`6f8ce6e`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/6f8ce6e89c5cb1a5195ccba764565dedbb19b6a3) Thanks [@l458022](https://github.com/l458022)! - Render: on Windows, WARP ("Microsoft Basic Render Driver", what a computer without a GPU driver or a remote session falls back to) counts as software, so `look` warns about it and render doesn't keep Vulkan on it.

- [#73](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/73) [`9018975`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/90189757998c75c68c4fa5c534dca363b8907768) Thanks [@HEOJUNFO](https://github.com/HEOJUNFO)! - On Windows, render, look and Studio draw WebGL on the GPU. Headless Chromium picked no graphics backend there and fell back to SwiftShader, which draws on the CPU; it now starts with Vulkan, reads the renderer once, and keeps Chromium's default when Vulkan doesn't start or is software too (a virtual machine, a CI runner, a computer without a Vulkan driver).

## 0.2.0

### Minor Changes

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - `openfilm capture <url> <file.png>` takes a picture of a live web page in the browser OpenFilm draws films in, with nothing to install: a product or a site shown as it is, sharp enough to fill the stage. It can take the whole page, one element, a phone-sized window or the dark theme, and click or hide things first.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - An official skill, `replicate`: give the agent a video and it makes yours like it, keeping its structure, rhythm, look and motion while the content is yours. It watches the reference whole and in stretches with `openfilm look`, starts from an example's source when one fits, writes a plan part by part, and compares the two side by side. It never reuses the reference's footage, music, voice or words.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Saved: your own library, the same in every project. The bookmark button beside Upload switches the media pane to it, and back; it is the same pane, so upload, folders, list and grid, filters, sort, search, rename, move and delete all work there, and anything can go in it: video, pictures, sound, documents, any file. Right-click a clip on the timeline or the picture, or a file of the project, and choose Add to Saved; with several clips selected, save them as one (they come back on tracks of their own, laid out as they were, links kept) or each alone. Drag anything from Saved onto the timeline, or press Enter: a file is imported into the project as one from your computer is, and a saved clip comes back as you saved it, with its place, its look and the changes made in its page, its files copied into the project, so changing it there never changes the saved one. Saved is a folder beside your projects (Movies/OpenFilm/Saved on a Mac), so you can also add to it from Finder. The media pane no longer has a close button: the assets button beside the chat opens and closes it.

  Template: select a page clip and the inspector shows what its page lets you change, without opening the page: each of its words, each of its pictures (replace one with a picture of the project, one dropped from the media pane, or one from your computer), and the CSS custom properties its stylesheet sets on `:root` (a color with a color picker, a number with its unit, a font with the font picker, words in quotes as text). Each change is kept for that clip only, and one changed can go back to the page's own. A page saved with such properties is marked as a template in Saved.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Nine more official skills, each with a version, so you can tell which one made a film:

  - Workflows: `long-to-short` (a long video cut into shorts that stand alone), `screen-demo` (a screen recording made into a polished demo), `motion-graphics` (studio-level motion graphics), `presenter-pip` (you speaking from a small window while the content fills the screen).
  - Styles: `whiteboard` (drawn by hand as a voice explains), `vintage-film` (photos as old film and instant prints), `lyric-video` (a song's lyrics as the picture, every word on the voice), `keynote` (a launch film with your real product on stage), `collage` (cut-paper motion graphics).

  A skill's openfilm.json can now give its `version`, its `kind` (`workflow` or `style`), its `order`, an `icon`, a `cover` picture and a `preview` video.

  The Community's skills are redesigned: a row of filters at the top (all, yours, this project's, workflows, styles) with adding a skill beside it; yours come first; a note on what skills are for; and a skill opens as a page with its picture or preview on the left and what it is, its version and using it on the right.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Tabs: the desktop window keeps each open project in a tab of its own, in Studio's top bar, side by side, the one in front in the panes' color. `+` (⌘T) opens the projects window, and the project picked opens in a new tab (or comes forward, when it is open already). ⌘W closes a tab, ⇧⌘T opens the last one closed again, ⌘1–9 and ⌃Tab switch. A tab shows when an agent is working in it, when one is waiting for your answer, and an export's progress; agents and exports go on in tabs behind. The tabs come back as you left them; with none open, the window shows your projects. The projects window has two views: your projects, and Examples, where example films play their preview under the pointer and open on a page of their own with their parts, to start a new film from. Skills show their own title and summary in your language (a skill's optional `openfilm.json`). An examples index can list each example's `category`, `segments` and a `video` preview; one read from a file has its pictures served by Studio. Studio in a browser keeps its projects window, now with the same two views.

### Patch Changes

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - In OpenFilm an agent keeps its maker's own MCP servers, but the ones you added to it yourself stay off until you turn each on. The switches are in Settings → Agents, under the agent; this covers Claude Code (its claude.ai connectors stay), Codex, OpenCode, Qwen Code, Gemini CLI and Copilot. Your own skills now stay off in Qwen Code and Gemini CLI too, and Copilot and Qwen Code get OpenFilm's skills. Claude Code's skill filter no longer waits for a first session to learn its skills.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - `openfilm get asr` now keeps the transcript beside any recording in the project, not only those under `assets/`. A file at the top of the project, or a reference video in `references/`, gets its WebVTT too. Before, none was kept and the answer wrongly said the service does not time words. In Studio, making subtitles now works for a clip whose file is outside `assets/`: before, it was transcribed on every try and nothing was kept. Files and folders starting with a dot (`.git`) belong to the tools, so a recording there is refused before anything is sent.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - When the preview can't be drawn in Safari or Firefox, Studio now says why: films are drawn and exported with Chromium, so a page may use something that browser doesn't have, and Chrome or Edge shows it. The README says which browsers to open Studio in.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a click on the picture takes what is under a full-frame overlay. When the clip on top has nothing there but a layer filling its page (a letterbox, a vignette, grain laid over the film), the click, the hover outline and a double-click to type go to the title or picture under it in the clip below. Before, the overlay's clip was taken and nothing under it could be picked in the picture.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: when Codex is installed more than once, as a command installed long ago and the copy inside the Codex or ChatGPT app (or a Microsoft Store app), the newer one is used: the models Codex offers depend on its version, so an old copy showed only a few. On Windows, a reason Codex could not start is readable instead of `������` on a system in Chinese or another non-English language.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: when Codex is found but can't start, the chat's model menu now says why, in Codex's own words, instead of only "Couldn't start it to read its settings". A Codex older than the chat needs says to update it. Settings → Agents no longer shows Codex as connected when it can't read its own config file, and names the setting it could not read.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: a file added to the message you are writing opens to look at, as one in a sent message does: click a video to play it, a picture to see it large, a text to read it.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: the files added to the message you are writing show in whole rows, up to three at once, so a message's twelve files are all in view. Before, the second row was cut in half and had to be scrolled to.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio's preview no longer flashes at a cut. A page coming on used to show before its frame was drawn, so for a moment it showed how it looked before: its first state, or the frame it drew the last time it was on. Now it shows once that frame is drawn, and the picture before it stays until then. Exports were never affected.

- [#47](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/47) [`e70d9d9`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e70d9d93c7bebe3d634ff2caa6f7a738cdde59d3) Thanks [@wang135531](https://github.com/wang135531)! - Speech recognition with Doubao using your Volcengine Speech API key, with word-timed transcripts from audio and video recordings.

  Keep transcript lines visible through their timed words, even when speech is slower than the estimated reading time.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - The Examples and the skills show at once. OpenFilm comes with a copy of the examples index and its posters, and keeps the last index it read, so the Examples tab no longer waits on GitHub (a slow or blocked connection showed nothing); the newest index is read in the background as Studio starts. A gallery opened again shows what it read before while it asks again, skill covers and example posters stay in the browser's cache, and the app reads what the composer's skills need as it starts.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: words typed into a line in the picture show in the inspector as they are typed. Its text box and the layer's name follow each key, and go back to the old words when the edit is given up with Esc. Before, the inspector kept the old words until Enter.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: Your own key works again. The 0.1.7 installer carried newer versions of the agent's packages than the ones tested, one of which no longer fit, so every message ended with "ACP connection closed". The installer now carries exactly the versions in the lockfile, and its build stops if any differ.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Settings → Agents: an agent that is connected shows Disconnect alone. Locate… is offered when it is disconnected (beside Connect) or not found.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a theme change reaches everything at once. The audio meters beside the timeline and the clips' waveforms are drawn again in the new theme's colors (before, they kept the other theme's, such as dark meter bars in the light theme, until the film played), and every other open page of Studio, such as another tab of the app, takes the theme too.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - A film with hundreds of sounds renders and exports on Windows. Its mix no longer puts everything on one command line, which Windows limits to 32,767 characters: a sound used by many clips is read once, and a long mix's graph goes in a file.

- [#53](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/53) [`b138595`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/b138595d4c2d1fa5262b4a8a8c7cd98187c4b077) Thanks [@HEOJUNFO](https://github.com/HEOJUNFO)! - A timecode typed into the inspector at 23.976, 29.97 or 59.94 fps lands on the frame that shows that timecode, instead of the frame before it, and a typed `12f` counts frames at the real rate.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Your own key's agent gets a shorter system prompt with no conflicting instructions. It was told both that it already had the manual and that it should print it, and both to open Studio and never to open it. Rules it got two or three times are now said once. Its own part is a third shorter, and the manual is unchanged.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: Your own key is an agent with models of your own, each set up on its own: the name the chat shows, the API format and address of its service, its own key, the model asked for there, and the reasoning efforts it takes. The chat's model menu switches between them, from one service to another, as it does for the other agents. A model on an address already set up can take that model's key. Models set up before become one each, on the service and key they had.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Settings: Models is now Services (which service makes each kind of media), and your own services moved there as Custom services. One is set up with its name, address, key and a description of what it does and how it is called (or where its API docs are): where the key goes is no longer asked, since your agent puts it where the service's docs say.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Settings say what is there now: Custom services take as many services as you like, and say you can also tell your agent about a service in the chat (one with a key is better set up there, so the key stays out of the conversation). The note pointing to the old "Your own services" is gone, Agents describes your own key as models you set up, Providers says the choice of each kind of media is under Services, and the sign-in wait no longer names Terminal (Windows opens PowerShell).

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Skills: ways of making a kind of film, each a folder with a SKILL.md and the files it brings. `openfilm skills` lists the skills a film can be made with: the project's own (in its `skills/` folder), yours (in `~/Movies/OpenFilm/Skills`), and the official ones. `openfilm skill <name>` prints one for the agent to follow. `openfilm skill add <GitHub address or folder>` adds a skill to yours and lists any scripts it brings. The manual tells agents about them. Studio no longer lists pages inside a project's `skills/` folder.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - In the chat, a skill can be named in a message: type `/`, or pick one in the Community; it goes in as a pill and the agent gets the skill's instructions with the message. The composer's Community button opens over your film on the skills (official, yours, this project's), with the examples beside them: point the agent at an example, or a part of one (a segment, or a stretch dragged on its strip), and it reads how that film is made; or start a new film from it, in a tab of its own. Add a skill there from a GitHub link or a folder, or make one with the agent. In OpenFilm an agent keeps its maker's own skills and gets OpenFilm's, but the skills you installed in it yourself stay off until you turn each on in Settings → Agents. This works for Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode and your own key's agent; agents that can't be told say they use all of yours. The context ring is gone: when a conversation is nearly full, a line above the composer offers a new one.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Your own skills are kept in `~/Movies/OpenFilm/Skills` (beside Projects and Exports, backed up with them); skills kept in `~/.openfilm/skills` move there on their own. A skill replaced or removed goes to the trash, never deleted, and `openfilm skill remove <name>` moves one there. Adding from a link or a folder shows what came first: each skill, its size, the scripts it brings and any of yours it would replace, then Add or Cancel. The switches for an agent's own skills are in Settings → Agents, beside its MCP servers. OpenFilm's own skill for terminal agents is no longer shown in the app.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a project with thousands of files plays smoothly as soon as it opens. While its files' lengths and sizes were read, each answer laid out the media pane again (tens of ms with that many files), so the picture stalled again and again for the first minutes. The answers now come in a few times a second, and the pane works out its folders in one pass.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: stopping the agent while a command it ran was still going no longer starts a turn of its own a moment later, "A background task finished; the agent went on", that Stop could not end. The rest of a command from a turn that already ended (its last output, its exit) now stays out of the chat. Stop ends a turn the agent started by itself at once. Such a turn that ends with nothing in it leaves no line behind, including ones already saved.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Choose where new projects go. Settings → General → Storage shows the folder, with the free space on its disk, and lets you change it (or go back to the default), for example to keep large projects off a full system disk. Exports go where you pick when exporting, as before. New projects, including ones fetched with `openfilm open`, go to the folder you choose; projects made before stay where they are and still open from the list. On Windows, the installer now asks where to install the app.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a short clip's thumbnails fill it. Each picture in a clip's row was squeezed to the clip's width while the pictures kept their places, so the clip's color showed between them and the row looked broken.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - A turn's log (Settings → Developer) now shows everything the agent and its model got. A new "Sent to the agent" tab shows how the app started the agent, every word it adds to the agent's own instructions, and each message exactly as the agent got it, with its pictures. Model requests are no longer only your own key's: for Claude Code, Codex and Kimi they are read from each agent's own record. Each request shows its full system prompt, its tools, every message (including what the agent added by itself: project instructions, environment, skill lists, reminders), the answer and its tokens. Subagents' requests are included. Turns from before this change show their requests too, as long as the agent's record is still there.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - A turn's log (Settings → Developer) now shows model requests for Gemini CLI, GitHub Copilot, Qwen Code, CodeBuddy and OpenCode too: each request's system prompt, tools, every message (including what the agent added by itself), the answer and its tokens. With Developer mode on, Gemini CLI, Qwen Code, CodeBuddy and OpenCode are started with their own local logging switch, written into a folder of the app's and kept for a week. The app leaves any telemetry you set yourself as it is. CodeBuddy is logged only when its own telemetry is off (`DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1`), since otherwise its traces would name each logged file. Copilot's requests are rebuilt from its own record, which has no tool definitions. Where a log lacks something, the viewer says so, and it explains a turn that has no requests (Cursor makes its prompt on its servers).

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - A turn's log shows how the session's skills and MCP servers were set: OpenFilm's skills, your own skills and servers kept off, and the ones you let in. A thought the agent did not keep in its record now says so, instead of showing 0 characters.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio run with npm updates itself: when a newer OpenFilm is out, Update appears in the top bar (and in Settings → About), and a click runs the newest in the background; Studio starts again on it and the page comes back on the new version. `openfilm update` does the same from a terminal, or for an agent asked to update OpenFilm. The desktop app's Studio still updates with the app.

- [#58](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/58) [`8ca326c`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/8ca326c24970fd6f639b3af064a0ea298ff2bdf6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - What's new: 0.1.7's notes, missing when it shipped, so Settings → About and Developer show them, and an update from 0.1.6 shows them once. Every version now has its notes before it can be released.

- [#49](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/49) [`b92c292`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/b92c29240b7fec2d9e993c23264eaf6ac593db5e) Thanks [@Abramfreemm](https://github.com/Abramfreemm)! - Preserve Unicode file and folder paths in Windows Studio dialogs so importing media from paths containing non-ASCII characters works correctly.

## 0.1.7

### Patch Changes

- [#40](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/40) [`be758b9`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/be758b9fe010d03bca33d250617e3e52f49d77f3) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: an agent's own notices show apart from its reply. A Codex warning such as "Falling back from WebSockets to HTTPS transport" is now a quiet line of its own instead of words in the middle of the answer, as are notes like "Context compacted".

- [#39](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/39) [`9c05395`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/9c05395202cdb42c706ddfb0de0d9faf3a7d40f6) Thanks [@youyouhdhd](https://github.com/youyouhdhd)! - Settings → Agents: an agent found and connected has Locate… too, beside Disconnect, so the program it runs can be chosen even when one was found — a particular version, another install. What was chosen still says so under the agent, with Find it automatically.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a title picked on the picture can be restyled together with every title like it. The inspector's "All like it" sends its color, size, font and the rest to each text of the same tag and classes in every page of the film (its words and place stay its own), in one change and one undo.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Your own key: each model has the reasoning efforts you give it. In Settings → Agents → Your own key, models are a list: add one by name, tick the efforts it takes (Auto, Off, Minimal, Low, Medium, High, Extra high, Max) and choose the one it starts on. The chat's model menu then offers those efforts for that model, as it does for Claude Code and Codex. Auto sends no reasoning setting and lets the service decide; a new model offers Auto alone, so nothing changes until you choose.

- [#40](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/40) [`be758b9`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/be758b9fe010d03bca33d250617e3e52f49d77f3) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: keys and clicks in the chat stay in the chat. A click anywhere in it, not only in the message box, keeps the keyboard there: Space no longer plays the film, and Delete, ⌘A or ⌘Z no longer act on Studio. Clicking into the chat keeps what is selected in Studio, so the message can point at it. Typing while a media file is previewed no longer plays it or loses the spaces.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: the caret moves freely around references in the message box. The arrow keys step over a reference in one press instead of stopping on it, where typing did nothing. A click between two references puts the caret there, and a click on one goes where it points with the caret left where it was, no longer thrown to the start of the message. References have a little more room around them.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: Claude Code's model menu shows its four current models, as Claude Code's own picker does (the one in use stays too, if it is an older one). With Claude Code both installed on its own and in the Claude app, the later of the two is used, so an install that was never updated no longer brings old model names.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: Claude Code runs on your Claude subscription only. An `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` or `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` in your shell is no longer given to it, so a stale key or gateway can no longer make it look connected and fail every message (a gateway of your own goes in Settings → Agents → Own key). Signing in uses the way Claude Code's adapter offers it (ACP terminal sign-in). Claude Code is connected when you connect it; Codex still is from the start. On Windows, signing in opens a PowerShell window that runs a script, so an install path with a space in it no longer breaks it.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: while a title is typed in, a click on its words puts the caret there, and a drag selects some of them. Something the page draws over the words unseen (a number still to come in, a grain) or a clip on top took that click, and the edit ended instead. A click anywhere else still keeps the edit.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a double-click on a title types in it even right after a click somewhere else. That click no longer counted as the double-click's first one, so the double-click was aimed at where it had been and nothing was typed in.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: an empty timeline shows one wide drop band across the tracks, with the hint at its start.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: clicking from one layer to another in the picture goes straight from one to the next in the inspector, without showing the film's settings in between. A click on nothing still shows them.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: several layers selected on the picture have handles on their box: the corners scale them together, the sides stretch them, and past a corner they turn about the box's middle (Shift: in steps of 15°). Each layer keeps its place in the group as it scales or turns, and the change is one step to undo.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: the timeline shows which track is the main track by its look, not by a name. The lowest picture track is drawn a little taller, on a band of its own shade, with the magnet by its badge (hover the head for "Main track"), so it is clear where clips close up when the magnet is on and nothing reads like a track name to rename or delete.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: markers are simpler. Add one with the marker button left of split, with M, or by right-clicking the ruler. Markers sit on their own row under the ruler (under the subtitle row when it shows), each a small tab pointing down at its moment with its name beside it. Hover one for its time and a dashed line through the whole timeline; drag it to move it, snapping to clip edges and the playhead with that line shown; click it to go there and open its card (name, color, delete). A click or drag on the row between markers moves the playhead, as in the tracks' empty space. M on a marker that is already there opens its card ready to name it. The old markers list in the toolbar is gone, and a name typed in the card is kept when the card closes.

- [#40](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/40) [`be758b9`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/be758b9fe010d03bca33d250617e3e52f49d77f3) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a clip dropped on the timeline of a film just made is saved at once. The timeline's thumbnails, clip posters, the media pane's covers and waveforms now take turns (web pages one at a time, as the server draws them), so they no longer hold every connection while an edit or an import waits; a film's thumbnails also come sooner.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: the inspector's menus show a choice on the picture before it is picked, as the font menu does. The font size presets, the weight, the blend mode and the stroke position are shown while hovered or reached with the arrow keys, the selection box following; a click keeps one, leaving the menu takes it back. The arrow keys in a menu (or any inspector field) no longer nudge the layer in hand as well.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: clips selected together and dragged along their track go where they are let go. Dragged further than the gap to the next selected clip, the one in hand counted that clip as one it landed on, went into insert, and the clips jumped back after the clip before them. With the main track magnet on, clips moved or ⌥-copied together by one that is not the first now go in at the insert point by the first of them, instead of cutting the clip before it.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Your own services: in Settings → Providers, add any service you run or use (your own voice, a transcription server on this computer, an API that isn't listed) with its address, its key if it takes one, and what it does. Your agent reaches it with `openfilm get call`: Studio adds the key (it never reaches the agent or the chat), and whatever the service answers comes back as it is, text and JSON for the agent to read, files saved under `assets/<service>/`.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: a reference no longer shows a locate icon on hover, since clicking it is what goes there. Its card now says so: "Click to jump to this reference".

- [#40](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/40) [`be758b9`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/be758b9fe010d03bca33d250617e3e52f49d77f3) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a clip's inspector has a Replace button: another file of the project in its place, or one picked from your computer (also in the clip's menu, under Replace with…). The media pane lists files in the order they were added, the newest last, and selects what you just imported. In the viewer, a click in the dark around the picture no longer pauses the film: it stops only once a selection box is pulled open there.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: restyling a layer no longer moves the others. A page often lines words up in a row (a number and the words beside it); a wider or larger font for the number pushed the words along. Now the layer takes its new size (its selection box grows with a larger font) while margins give the page back the room it gained, written with its style, so the words beside it, under it or lined up with its baseline stay where they are, while the menu previews a font too and when its line height changes. A width or height you set yourself still moves what is around it.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: the ruler points at time for the chat. A click on it puts that moment in the message, a drag that span: the moment under the pointer is drawn as a line with a tag of its time and the chat's mark, a drag as its span; the playhead stays where it is (its knob drags it, a click in the tracks' empty space moves it). A clip or a subtitle line referred to the chat no longer shows its time on its pill (it is on its card): a pill shows time only when it is time.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - The OpenFilm on the web (`npx openfilm`) and the app share their projects list, keys and preferences on this computer. When their versions differ, one no longer drops what only the other knows (a newer version's settings) as it saves.

- [#33](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/33) [`9d82e89`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/9d82e89700d9cd0749e93de2ec0775f0b315efcd) Thanks [@xyd945](https://github.com/xyd945)! - A sound's file is always read from inside the film's folder. A path with a slash written as `%2F` was decoded after the URL had resolved its `..`, so a page's script could name a file above the folder and `look`, `render` and Studio's exports read it; such a path, or a link that leads out of the folder, is now refused with its name. A link to media the person linked (Studio's linked places) is still read, as the file server reads it. A sound's timing and volume have to be numbers before they go into ffmpeg's filters.

- [#40](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/40) [`be758b9`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/be758b9fe010d03bca33d250617e3e52f49d77f3) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: pressing a subtitle on the picture, or its line on the timeline's subtitle row, opens the subtitle style (font, size, color, outline, box) in the inspector, as pressing a layer opens its own.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a title restyled keeps a box that hugs its words. A heading as wide as the card it sits in kept that width, so a smaller size left its selection box far wider than its words; and the room kept for the words beside it after one change stayed for the next and stretched it further. Now each change works out that room again, and a preview taken back shows exactly what is kept.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a page clip's pictures on the timeline show the changes made inside it. A layer moved, restyled or retyped on the picture was not in them, since they were drawn from the page's file alone; now they are drawn with the clip's changes, and drawn again after each one.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a title written with its subtitle inside it (`<div>Title<div>subtitle</div></div>`) can be picked in the picture, and only its own words are picked. Its box hugs its words and the inspector shows them; a double-click types them in place while the subtitle stays shown. Moved, scaled, turned or restyled, the title leaves its subtitle where it was and as it looked: the subtitle's own override that keeps it there is written with the title's edit, one undo. The subtitle is still a layer of its own.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: undoing a delete that emptied a whole track puts that track back as it was. Before, every clip of it came back on a new track of its own, so the timeline looked scrambled; tracks emptied together come back in their order too.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - A voice-over made again under the same name (by a service that doesn't time its words, or one of your own services) no longer keeps the last take's subtitles: they go to the trash, so subtitles are never timed by another take, and your agent is told the new take has none yet.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop on Windows: no more command windows flashing up. Checking the agents, reading Codex's plan, talking to Claude Code or Codex and checking ffmpeg ran console programs that each opened a window, and Settings → Agents, which looks again whenever the window gets focus, kept opening them one after another. They now run hidden; only the sign-in window shows.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: new words for a title leave the others where they are, as a restyle does. Typed in place or in the inspector, longer or shorter words pushed what sits beside or under them; now the title's box takes the words (hugging them) and keeps its place in the page, as each key is typed too.

- [#46](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/46) [`e4e67fe`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4e67fe860048be1df2c6135c805cc773d85ca98) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a click on a title takes the title, though a line of a drawing behind it runs nearby. A line was found by the box around it, so a diagonal line in the background took clicks meant for the words beside it, and a double-click there typed nothing.

## 0.1.6

### Patch Changes

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Settings → About in the desktop app shows the version and its update without going to the menu: it looks for one on opening (unless it looked a few minutes ago), shows "Up to date" with when it last checked, or the new version downloading with its progress, then "Restart to update" once it is ready (it installs at the next quit too). A failed check says why, with Try again. A build run from source says it doesn't update itself. The menu's Check for Updates… is still there.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: when a background task of the agent's own ends after its turn (a command Claude Code ran in the background, a subagent) and it takes it up by itself, the chat shows that work as a turn of its own, under "A background task finished; the agent went on", with its log; before, it was lost. It ends with the agent's result, and as soon as you write. A ring beside the composer's tools shows how full the agent's context is in the conversation, with the numbers on hover. The `/` menu has the agent's commands from the start (they were there only after a first message), keeps them between launches, finds them by name, and shows each name whole.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: the agent's own commands in the chat, as in its own: type `/` at the start of a message for the list Claude Code or Codex gives (their built-in commands and your own skills and custom commands), with what each does; it goes to the agent as typed.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: each conversation in the chat is its own session of the agent's, and it goes on where it stopped after a restart, a reload, or switching conversations: the chat keeps the agent's own session id and resumes it, as `claude --resume` or `codex resume` would. A new conversation starts a new session (it carried the last one's context before). A session left idle for 20 minutes closes, and its next message resumes it. A picture attached to a message is sent to the agent beside the text, as one pasted in its own chat is, besides being put in the project. The message goes as written (the first one no longer starts with a line about the language), so a slash command typed first works.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: the chat's agents are told to use the app's own `openfilm` command, not `npx openfilm` (which an installed OpenFilm skill says), so they work on the Studio in the window at its version; and only not to open Studio in a browser, rather than never to open a browser.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: the model menu has the models the agent offers now. It asks each agent for its options once a launch (what it had from before shows meanwhile), so an updated Claude Code's models replace the ones an earlier version had; and a pick it no longer offers is dropped for the agent's own default.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: write to Claude Code or Codex while it works, as in its own chat. The composer's button turns from Stop to Send once something is written, and the message goes into the running turn (the agent reads it at its next step); it shows as a message of its own, with what the agent does from then on under it. An agent that cannot take a message mid-turn gets it as soon as the turn ends, as before. Esc still stops.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: less work around a running agent. Studio no longer redraws the film when the agent writes a file no page loads (its notes, scripts, logs). The chat around a turn no longer re-renders for every word the agent streams. A model menu whose agent did not answer is asked again only when the menu opens or on Retry, not on every render (each try was a whole session of the agent's). A turn's log open while it runs is read again only once it has grown.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Settings → Agents shows whose account each of your agents uses (organization, email and plan for Claude Code; the ChatGPT account for Codex). The agents use the account signed in on your computer: to use another, switch accounts in the agent itself, then disconnect and connect it again in OpenFilm.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: Claude Code and Codex in the chat run as they do in your terminal. They get everything your shell sets (an API key or a gateway such as ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL, an MCP server's token, your certificates, your ssh agent), where before they got only its PATH. Their model and reasoning effort are your own config's until you pick another in the menu (the chat no longer sets High). Codex keeps your own developer_instructions, with OpenFilm's after them. Your ffmpeg and Playwright browsers come first for the commands they run; the app's ffmpeg and browser go only to the `openfilm` command (and ffmpeg last on the PATH, for a computer that has none). Electron's ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE no longer reaches their commands. A session starts without waiting on your shell to be read again.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: Codex, Claude Code and the other agents are found on Windows where they were missed. The PATH is read as Windows has it now, so an agent installed after signing in to Windows is found without signing out; the folders of pnpm, Volta, nvm-windows, Yarn, Bun, Scoop, Chocolatey and winget are searched, with npm's own global folder; and the shims npm, pnpm and Yarn write are read correctly (npm's current shim was taken for its node.exe). A shim that cannot be read is started through cmd.exe. On a Mac too, the login shell's PATH is read again while the app runs, so an agent installed meanwhile is found without restarting the app.
  Settings → Agents: an agent not found has Locate…, which opens the file dialog; the program chosen is tried (it has to answer `--version`) and used from then on, until Find it automatically. "Why isn't it found?" says what to do (installed only in WSL, files that cannot start, nothing found) and copies a report of every folder searched, to send with an issue.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: the model menu has no Mode: every agent works with full access, and a plan mode picked there before is no longer applied.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Settings → Agents says "Not signed in" for an agent that has never been signed in on the computer, instead of "Signed out".

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Settings → Agents shows what is left of Codex's plan without opening it: each window (five hours, this week) with a bar of how much is used and when it resets, as Codex itself says, and its account's email and plan. Claude Code's is not shown: its own report said 0% of a Team account the Claude app showed half used.

- [#24](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/24) [`f91edd3`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/f91edd3151989655bc7fe42304aa4d63f1a4fb27) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: changing the canvas's shape fits what the film shows to the new canvas, centered, with at least one side meeting its edge. Shapes picked one after another no longer shrink the film further each time, and a film already shrunk grows back to the edge on the next change.
  The ratio menu lists plain ratios (and 21:9), with "Original" first and nothing else, and the inspector drops its note about the canvas. A project opens with the media, the viewer, the inspector and the timeline shown, and the layers hidden.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a canvas change is applied to the picture in place, without loading the film again, so the film no longer jumps to the top left before it settles in the middle. Dragging the canvas's width or height in the inspector changes the picture as the mark moves. The picture no longer shows a light hairline along its right and bottom edges in the dark theme.
  Studio's address keeps its key (`?key=…`), so an address copied from the address bar opens Studio in any browser on this computer, or in an agent's preview, without being signed out. The picture is clipped to its own box, so no line shows along its edges. The inspector is open whenever a project opens. A canvas change fits each picture to the new canvas on its own, so a portrait photo on one clip no longer shrinks 16:9 footage on another. Dragging the canvas's width or height shows the size and shape on the picture and in the inspector as it moves (Custom unless it is a preset). A click on a page's empty part selects the whole page, so a page smaller than the canvas can be moved and sized on the picture. A canvas side typed or dragged stays from 16 to 8192 px and within 1:4 to 4:1. With a whole page in hand, a click on something in it selects that (a drag still moves the page). ⌘A (Ctrl+A) selects every layer on the picture; Shift-click and a drag on empty space select several, as before. Everything a click can select is outlined under the pointer: a page's layers, a whole page by its empty part, a video or a still, with a clip in hand too. A marquee around whole clips on the picture (by the part on the canvas) selects those clips; one inside a page selects its layers, as before. While a marquee is drawn, what letting go would select is outlined as it goes. Several clips selected have one frame around them all, and a drag on one of them moves them all, written in one step. The frame around several clips can be dragged anywhere inside it, empty space too, and its corners scale them all in proportion; its size shows below it. Several clips' frame also turns (its corner rings, Shift in 15° steps) and, for videos, stretches from its sides; the arrow keys move them all.
