Several layers selected show their shared looks in the inspector (type, appearance, adjustments, effects), and let go when the timeline is pressed or the playhead leaves their clip, as one layer does. A clip selected on the timeline away from the playhead has no frame on the picture until the playhead reaches it. A new mask has a hard edge (no feather). The inspector's menus (blend mode, font weight) and the export's range ends open Studio's own dark menu instead of the system's light list. The inspector does more: several things' X, Y, W and H are their bounds, typed to move or scale them together; Appearance has corner radius, one for all or each corner its own; Fill is a section of its own (a line's color, a box's background, with + and −); Stroke is too, drawn as an outline that adds nothing to the box, inside, centered or outside the edge; Effects add an inner shadow, a layer blur and a background blur beside the drop shadow and faded edges; type can be underlined or struck through. Under a mask, a crop or faded edges, which cut away what is past the edge, the stroke offers Inside only (a stroke already placed otherwise shows where it is, with a note). Setting one corner keeps the others; removing a fill leaves the layer see-through (not the page's own fill back) and still selectable; an SVG shape's fill can be set; an image's Fit button fits it. A clip that plays all of its file shows its in point grayed out, with a note to shorten it first. The Mask button on the picture's selection bar opens a menu of shapes (the one on now checked): the one picked is put on the clip, its handles on the picture. A clip's fades sit right under its position and size in the inspector. A marquee can start on the dark around the picture, so what sits at its edge can be boxed whole; a click there lets go of what is in hand.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: more keyboard shortcuts, and paste attributes. ⌘⇧C copies a clip's attributes and ⌘⇧V pastes them onto the selected clips, with a dialog to pick position and size, blending, volume and fades (also in the clip's menu). P turns the main-track magnet on or off, ~ linkage, ⇧X sets in and out to the selected clips, ⌥X clears them, ⌘R opens the clip's speed, ⇧⌃S detaches a video's sound, ⌥⇧M goes to the previous marker, and ⇧← / ⇧→ move the playhead 10 frames.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: a conversation's messages are kept as the agent works, not only once its turn ends. Quitting the app, a crash or a restart in the middle of a turn no longer loses it: it comes back as it was, marked Interrupted, and the next message goes on from there (the agent resumes its own session, so it still has everything it did). Turns lost this way before (their logs were kept) come back when their project opens.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a click on the empty part of a page takes the clip, so a drag moves the whole picture. A box that fills the whole page (a page's backdrop) is no longer taken instead; it is still selected in Layers, or with Alt.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: "Close gap" always closes the gap picked, including the empty time before a track's first clip. With sync lock on, when a clip on another track leaves no room to move everything together, the gap closes on its own track alone and a notice says so. It used to do nothing.

- [#29](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/29) [`2a10779`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/2a10779601380805a0c1378f302396bcb6b140bb) Thanks [@Thebearcoding](https://github.com/Thebearcoding)! - Collect linked folders as local copies so their files remain available when the original disk or NAS is disconnected.

- [#28](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/28) [`80f1185`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/80f11856b34e4509bf26b178baa8cbbbc5c70543) Thanks [@Thebearcoding](https://github.com/Thebearcoding)! - Keep distinct files when same-named attachments are imported at once, and reuse identical uploads without leaving extra copies.

- [#23](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/23) [`e688860`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e688860dec6717c4c67b5cf26b920f5b3d9ce416) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: the Desktop dialog is laid out again, with the app's window and the agents it works with beside eight clear points (two-way chat with the editor, live changes, media dropped in, any agent, faster preview and export, no terminal or dependencies, updates, kept chats) and the download. A layer selected on the picture lets go when the timeline is pressed or the playhead leaves its clip, and the picture no longer gets a ring of its own while something is selected. The bar over a selection shows at once, without rising. The subtitle row's menu can hide it, and says where it comes back. The export's "Save to" is one button with the folder's name; in the desktop app it opens the system's folder dialog instead of a typed path. A track's name shows under its badge, so the track heads keep their width. The examples follow the Projects view (cards or rows). A media folder just named stays in place until the listing has it, and a click on empty space in the media pane lets go of what is selected. The Desktop dialog's download sits bottom right beside the version, and its agents' names show on hover. Settings → Models shows a choice only while its provider is connected. Files dragged from the computer onto a media folder go into that folder. A page previewed in the media pane starts at 0 when another is picked. The projects' sort by name says so, a folder's name sits in the Projects title (its projects start where the list does), folders in the list view show their covers, and removing a project whose folder is gone says that nothing of it is left on disk. Projects folders can hold folders, with the trail to them in the title. The media pane can show a list as well as a grid, says why a web page cannot go into a folder, and no longer picks a file when a drag starts. The export's "Save to" opens the system's folder dialog in a browser too (Studio opens it on this computer), exports go to the Desktop by default, and the frame rate is picked from an editor's list (10 to 60 fps, 23.976, 29.97, 59.94, and 120). `pnpm dev` refuses to start a second time for the same checkout. Studio works inside another app's frame (an agent's preview pane): its sign-in has a partitioned twin a frame keeps, and the film's pages no longer refuse to be framed there. A clip dragged onto another clip of its own track goes in between them, as in an editor's main track, instead of cutting it (⌥ still overwrites). A transcript that runs past the end of its file (made from another version of it, such as one sped up in place) is listed to be made again, so its subtitles no longer drift and lose their end. A browser that opens Studio's address without its key is told so, and which address lets it in, instead of a page that seems to reconnect forever. The manual tells agents to change a clip's speed only with `speed`, so its subtitles stay with it. A media file opens playing from its start, with one full-screen button in its bar. Ratios add 3:4, 3:2, 2:3, 2.39:1, 1.85:1 and 2:1 and any ratio typed from 1:4 to 4:1. The project's frame rate is picked from the same list as the export's (23.976 to 60, 48 and 120), in Studio's own menu. Web pages can be moved into folders like any media: their links (and those of every page pointing at what moved) are written again; paths a page makes while it runs are named before the move. The viewer's ratio button and the inspector change the film's canvas itself, at any time: the usual shapes (and Fit to the first picture, and any typed size), the canvas's short side kept, and the film scaled into it whole, centered, in one undoable step; the inspector gives the words that ask the agent to lay the film out again. A delivery frame (bars or a crop) is now the export's alone. The export dialog's destinations scroll, so its note is never cut. A web page that sets no size of its own keeps the size it was laid out at when the canvas changes shape (film.html's new `size` on a page clip, which Studio writes), so it is scaled into place like a video instead of being laid out again smaller.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Export › Social platforms in Chinese offers China's platforms first: 哔哩哔哩 (1080p, 60 fps), 抖音, 快手 and 视频号 (1080 × 1920) and 小红书 (3:4, 1080 × 1440), beside the others; the first time, 哔哩哔哩 is picked. Export › Project takes only the work unless more is asked each time: the work is always taken and shown on, the version history no longer starts on, and a switch's words are lit only when it is on.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Export › Editing project is for one app at a time, each by its own name: 剪映 (CapCut outside Chinese), Premiere Pro or DaVinci Resolve. For 剪映 or CapCut the timeline goes straight into the app's drafts folder and opens there as a project, ready to keep cutting: web pages as clips with alpha, footage placed where it is in the film with its own sound, other sounds with their levels and fades, and subtitles as a text track. When that app is on the computer it is the choice the dialog starts with; Premiere Pro and DaVinci Resolve get the XML, each with its own import steps. The draft is for whichever of 剪映 and CapCut is on the computer (by language only when both or neither are), named as it is in every language. Every file of the draft is in the app's media panel, as if imported there.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: the export dialog says which media files are missing before anything is queued, and holds back exports that draw or play the film until they are relinked (a project, subtitles or an editing project still export). The timeline's toolbar is simpler: three switches: main-track magnet, snapping and linkage, each with a one-line tip. Sync lock is gone (linkage does its job), and a video and its detached sound are always selected together (⌥-click picks one).

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Export: a frame comes out in its exact shape, so 16:9 is 1920 × 1080 (and 4K 3840 × 2160) around any film, not 1918 × 1080 around a 2:1 one. "Change…" for where exports go opens the folder dialog in front, and the file dialog for importing media too: on macOS they opened behind the browser, so nothing seemed to happen. If macOS doesn't let Studio ask Finder, you can type the folder instead. The export list starts with Video file, then Editing project (what was Editing software), then Social platforms.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: getting a film from GitHub works like a download. A public repository comes over https, without git, so its size is known from the start: the bar shows how much of how much has come, how fast, and how long is left, and a dropped connection is tried again. Opening an example shows just its download and Cancel. Cancel stops it at once and leaves nothing behind (before, it started again straight away). A private repository, or one on GitLab, still comes through git. A download is stopped only when nothing has come for two minutes, not after ten minutes in all, so a big film on a slow line gets there.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - "Change…" for where exports go no longer opens a folder dialog that closes at once: the dialog starts in the folder shown, or the nearest one above it that is there yet (the exports folder is made by the first export), and Finder is no longer asked to find that folder itself. The same for importing and linking media. The export dialog's choices are a capsule inside a capsule, an even gap all round, and the layout menu's rows round as the menu does. The export's custom frame is one more chip in the row of frames, its width and height capsules inside it.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: four more ways to edit on the timeline. With the split tool, ⇧-click cuts every track at that point. ⌥-drag copies a clip (or the selection it is in): the clip stays and a copy lands where you let go, inserted on the main track and on a free track elsewhere. Clips selected together and dragged above or below every track open new tracks there, one for each track they came from. With linkage on, deleting a main-track clip deletes the titles, B-roll and sound riding on it; a music bed under several shots stays. Slip and slide are no longer ⌥-drag gestures; the inspector's source bar still slips a clip.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: more timeline changes. With the main-track magnet on, a paste covers and pushes nothing: a copy whose time is taken goes to a free track, or a new one right above the main track, and onto the main track only past its end. The empty timeline's menu gets "Close all gaps on this track". A freeze frame lasts 3 s.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a few more timeline changes. ⌘B with nothing selected splits the main track's clip under the playhead, and after a split the right half is the one selected. Turning the main-track magnet on closes the gaps already on the main track. A click on the ruler or the empty timeline while playing pauses again. After Q the playhead goes to the start of the trimmed clip. On the main track with the magnet on, dragging where two clips meet trims one of them and closes up what follows; there is no roll edit there.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: opening or closing a step list in a reply no longer shows "Jump to latest" when nothing is below the view. The button shows only once content actually goes below, and if the view is still at the bottom it keeps following the reply.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: the line under a reply (copy, the agent and its model, and in developer mode Log) also shows while the turn runs, on hover under its status. Its Log is the turn so far and keeps reading as it goes, the newest model request first, until the turn ends; it stays open as the turn finishes. Settings: Developer moves under Help, and the foot of the list links to the code on GitHub, the examples and the website.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: the timeline has a main track. The magnet is now the main-track magnet, on by default: the main track (the lowest picture track) stays closed up, so a clip put down there inserts, a delete or a clip dragged off it closes the gap, and trims ripple. Other tracks stay free: a clip put down there overwrites and a delete leaves a gap.
  A new Linkage switch, on by default, carries titles, B-roll and sound with the main-track clip they start over when that clip moves, is deleted, trimmed or sped up. A long clip under several shots, such as a music bed, stays where it is, and a carried clip that would run into another moves to a free track. Sync lock is still there, now off by default. ⌘B and Q / W with nothing selected act on the main track.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: adding, moving, naming or removing a marker is a step of the edit history, so ⌘Z undoes it and ⌘⇧Z makes it again, in turn with the film's edits.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: media kept elsewhere can stay there. Upload offers "Import files…" (a copy in the project, cloned where the disk can: at once, and no room taken), "Link files…" and "Link a folder…" (a link in assets/ to where the media is kept, a NAS or another disk). In the app, a file dropped from Finder is copied on the computer rather than sent through the page. A linked file or folder is marked; one that cannot be reached (the NAS is off) says "Not found". Its menu has "Show original", "Relink…" and "Copy into project"; deleting a link never touches the original. Studio follows a link out of a project only to a place the person linked on this computer, so a project from elsewhere cannot reach their other files; the preview, `openfilm look` and `openfilm render` read linked media, and nothing is written there.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a still or video dropped on the timeline keeps its own shape, as large as the canvas holds and centered, so its box is the picture and needs no crop. A phone's portrait video is read at the size it is shown.
  The media pane takes any kind of file (a PDF, a document, a model): what Studio cannot play shows with its icon and ending, under a new "Other" filter. Several files and folders can be picked at once (⌘-click, ⇧-click, a box dragged over empty space, ⌘A) and moved or deleted together; every file and folder has "Move to" on its right-click menu.
  A web page that sets no size of its own keeps the size it shows alone (1920 × 1080) when dropped, scaled whole into the canvas, instead of being laid out again in the canvas's shape. A page put in a media folder keeps its poster and plays as a page. The list view shows each file's type, dimensions, size and length as the pane widens; the tiles lose their "+" (drag a file to the timeline, or press Enter); the filter and sort menus close when their button is clicked again. The top bar fills its width: narrowing, Upload becomes its icon first, then the search field shortens, and only at the narrowest is the search a button that opens over the bar.
  An import shows a card for each file where it goes, its own progress inside (a picture shows itself while it is sent), in place of the bar above the media; a folder it goes into shows the progress on its cover.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: heavy footage plays from a proxy. Footage past 1080p, or slow to decode (ProRes, HEVC, 10-bit), gets a lighter copy made in the background (H.264, the same frames at the same times, a key frame every half second), and the preview plays it once it is ready; the media pane shows it being made, then marks it. Settings → General → Playback turns proxies off and sets their size (540p, 720p by default, 1080p). The film's sound, `openfilm render` and every export read the originals; a video's length and size are always the original's.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: with the main-track magnet on, clips on the other tracks never cover each other. A clip dragged, pasted, duplicated or dropped from the media pane where another one already is goes to the nearest free track of its kind, or a new one opens for it, instead of cutting what was there. Nudging with ⌥← / ⌥→ stops at a neighbor. Hold Alt while dropping to overwrite anyway.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: the timeline no longer offers fixed transitions. A click or right-click on a cut opens no menu (dragging it still rolls the cut), and a clip's menu has no "transition at its start / end". Transitions are your agent's to write; ones a film already has are still drawn, and can be made longer or shorter or removed. Fade in and fade out stay in the clip's menu.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - A film with many pages keeps only the eight nearest the playhead open, and opens the others as the playhead comes
  near. A film of more than 16 WebGL pages no longer shows its first scenes blank, in the preview or in an export.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a project with a missing media file plays again. The clip whose file is gone keeps its time and shows as black, the rest of the film plays and is heard as before, and the viewer says which file is missing while the playhead is over it, with a hint to relink it from the timeline. `openfilm check` and exports still stop at a missing file and name it.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a click on the ruler or the empty timeline while the film plays moves the playhead and playing goes on; dragging the playhead still pauses. With snapping on, the dragged playhead snaps to clip edges and markers, and a clip dragged onto the playhead shows the snap guide there too.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: [ selects every clip with a part before the playhead and ] every clip with a part after it, on tracks not locked.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - A sound in any folder named sfx, music or vo (such as assets/sfx/) is read as an effect, music or a voice. A sound effect no longer counts as speech with no subtitles.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - A `text` override keeps line breaks: on an element of words alone, or of lines between `<br>`s, it replaces all of the words, a `<br>` at each line break (SPEC §1). In Studio, a double-click on such a line types in it in place, its lines and width kept; Shift+Enter breaks a line, Enter keeps the words, Esc gives them up.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a trim that reaches the end of the clip's media says so beside the edge ("End of the media"), so it is clear why the edge stops. The snapping switch's tip now says what it snaps to: clip edges, the playhead and markers.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: under each reply, the files the turn added, deleted or changed, as a coding agent's app lists them: a row each with its lines added and removed, and on a click its changed lines. The first four show, the rest open from "N more files". Settings → Developer turns it off. It replaces the "Changed N clips" line developer mode showed.

- [#26](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/26) [`e0a80d5`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e0a80d515fa13457ece08925575d5453c0b0fc6d) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - A video frame that is decoded but never shown (a seek made just after its file loaded) is sought again, so a render, an export or `look` no longer takes the clip's first frame for the one asked for.

- [#31](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/31) [`9e68b61`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/9e68b610158aa309492d4bade96aba7f20f2a6e2) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - A video clip's first frame in a render, an export or `look` is the one asked for. A video loaded ahead of its cut, hidden, and sought as it came on could keep the frame it had for a second or so, though the browser said the new one was shown: the same moment drew two pictures. It is now shown before it is sought.

- [#22](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/22) [`387d392`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/387d3922b8a299c002963794aa9a2c807c865ef4) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Exact frames (render, look, Studio's exports): a video's frame on screen is told by how long its frames last, which the host reads with ffprobe. A video woken ahead while hidden, then shown, no longer leaves its old frame in the picture: shown again after the seek, it was taken for the sought one, and an export could hold a frame from the clip's start. Footage whose next frame is the same source frame (24 fps footage in a 30 fps export) no longer waits seconds for a frame that never comes: such a film exports several times as fast.

- [#27](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/27) [`2ddba70`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/2ddba704a4b3a9d46c5458b5b02fb8cf5bc192d8) Thanks [@Thebearcoding](https://github.com/Thebearcoding)! - Studio: voice-over WAV durations no longer count metadata after the audio as extra playback time. Streamed and truncated files still use only the audio bytes available.

- [#32](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/32) [`60cb113`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/60cb1133417d23eeb3da15190c29932ce8d6f021) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - After an update, OpenFilm says what is new, once: a few lines in your language, grouped as new, improved and fixed, with a link to the full changelog. A new install shows nothing. Settings → About shows this version's notes again, and Settings → Developer previews the next release's.

## 0.1.5

### Patch Changes

- [#18](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/18) [`10709ae`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/10709aeec857335c23fad514f9a0233c0fedaf14) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: a new version, once downloaded, is offered in the top bar beside the projects button (Restart to update) instead of in the chat, where a closed chat hid it. A restart that has to wait (an agent working, an export running) says why beside it.

- [#8](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/8) [`6a1d9b8`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/6a1d9b8363b967f985df6d11b2714d158b4ce245) Thanks [@lubobill1990](https://github.com/lubobill1990)! - Studio: Azure Speech (Azure AI Speech) can be connected under Settings → Providers for voice-over with its neural voices. The key carries the resource's region (`<region>:<key>`, or `AZURE_SPEECH_KEY` with `AZURE_SPEECH_REGION`), and one without a region is refused where it is pasted; Azure times no words, so subtitles spread the text over the line, as with OpenAI's voices.

- [#5](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/5) [`e4679d2`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4679d2d822552bae51ffdd7255ce089bd7c22c4) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Desktop: your own key can name several models (Settings → Agents, separated by commas), each checked for reading pictures; the chat's menu switches between them. The menu no longer stays on "Loading" for an agent with nothing to choose.

- [#15](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/15) [`12d34cd`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/12d34cdb89e1f9e234f005eee3c749466c21d824) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Faster exports that use more of the computer. A video whose key frames are far apart (most camera, phone and stock footage) gets a copy that seeks fast before a render, kept in the project's cache; the pages draw from the copy and the sound still comes from the original. A film with footage renders about three times as fast. Each of a render's pages gets a browser of its own, and a render uses a browser for every two threads with 3 GB of memory each, up to eight (it was up to four, sharing one browser). H.264 and HEVC below master go through the graphics chip's encoder when it has one that works: the Mac's for HEVC too, and on Windows NVIDIA's, Intel's, AMD's or Windows' own. Each one is tried on a moment of video first, and an export it fails partway is made again without it.

- [#13](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/13) [`74fd6d1`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/74fd6d1faa8bfb8ce88697398b6f4dac91549082) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Export: in the desktop app, the export tray (what an export is doing, how far it has got) sits beside the chat instead of under it; pressing Export seemed to do nothing while the film rendered unseen. An ffmpeg that ends before the last frame (not found, an encoder that fails to start) fails the export with ffmpeg's own error; the export used to stay at "preparing" forever. The export dialog finds an ffmpeg that arrives after Studio started (the desktop app downloads it on a first start); every format used to say this computer's ffmpeg could not make it until Studio restarted.

- [#11](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/11) [`70602ab`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/70602ab4bef7ec3b8a9621d88e3734717182213c) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: a key that Settings → Providers refuses says why under its own field, not at the bottom of the list past every provider.

- [#9](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/9) [`acae857`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/acae857fe2741163ebcc7e8dbaf54d01bdb6402b) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: Brave Search, Groq, Pexels, Pixabay and Tavily show their own logos in Settings → Providers, in place of a letter or a glyph of ours.

- [#5](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/5) [`e4679d2`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/e4679d2d822552bae51ffdd7255ce089bd7c22c4) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Studio: the subtitle panel's language and typeface menus are Studio's own, in its colors, grouped and with the chosen one checked, in place of the browser's list, which was light in a dark Studio and read as if every choice were disabled.

- [#12](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/pull/12) [`7f21b61`](https://github.com/openfilm/openfilm/commit/7f21b61f84467437614a7cefe40bf3582da257b6) Thanks [@PazChen](https://github.com/PazChen)! - Exact frames (render, look, Studio's exports): a video sought as it comes on waits for its own frame on screen. On a slow machine the old frame, shown again as the video turned visible, could be taken for it, and `look` reported the same t drawing two different pictures.

## 0.1.4

### Patch Changes

- f486188: Windows: an ffmpeg that is stopped (a cancelled render or export, a mix that runs too long) ends with the real ffmpeg
  behind a Chocolatey or Scoop shim; a name with a `:` is cleaned on import, rename and new folders as on macOS and
  Linux, not refused; Studio's supervisor opens the null device by its Windows name.
- A render or `look` frame of a video clip woken ahead of its cut waits for the seek it was woken with before its own:
  on a slow machine the frame showed the clip's first second instead of the one asked for, and `look` said the same t
  drew two pictures.

## 0.1.3

The first release from the open-source repository: the CLI, Studio and the desktop app share this version.

- Media comes only from services you connect with your own key, in Studio's Settings → Providers or from the environment. There are more of them, at least one for every kind of media: ElevenLabs, OpenAI, Google Gemini and fal.ai, now with Groq (fast transcripts with Whisper, voice-over with Orpheus), Pexels and Pixabay (image search), Brave Search and Tavily (web search and reading pages); fal.ai also makes music and sound effects. `openfilm get` names the services that make each kind you have not connected yet.
- Settings → Providers: every service is Connect or Disconnect. A key pasted in Studio wins over one in the environment, which connects its service unless disconnected.
- Studio's Projects page starts from an existing film: Import from a folder, or from GitHub (a repository or a folder in one, without its git history), and a row of example films. `openfilm open <github url>` does the same. A project whose folder moved can be located again.
- A Studio started by `openfilm open` that ends unexpectedly starts again by itself, on the same address; pages reconnect.
- Settings → General shows Studio's version and checks npm for a newer one.
- The Desktop dialog shows what the app does and the agents it works with, and downloads the installer for this computer.
- The desktop app's chat runs your own agents (Codex, Claude Code, Gemini CLI, GitHub Copilot, Cursor, OpenCode and others) or its own agent on your own API key.
